R03-ADDITIVE-CONTRIBUTION-BYTES retains fixed ONE/ONE accumulation for the
ordinary source-alpha/one RGB and one/one alpha recipe. The existing sampled
colour is premultiplied and truncated to byte contributions before blending;
full-alpha samples retain their original output. This avoids rounding one
extra level into every overlapping partial-alpha draw,without destination
snapshots,CPU colour evaluation or CPU raster changes. Other blend recipes
retain their existing factors and quantisation. Individual draws remain
TOLERANCE±2 and composed gameplay TOLERANCE±4 are diagnostic classes, with exact placement. The
432-control public additive-stack companion compares every prefix of ten
nearest sprites over six policies,four owners,world/GUI and source/draw alpha,
with explicit CPU boundaries,ordinary-transport and detached-state gates.
It is a finite regression domain,not full function or filtered qualification.


# Renderer backend correspondence

The CPU executor is the default renderer and the exact reference for runtime
state. The optional GPU executor uses the device's own filtering, blending and
program execution and is not required to reproduce CPU pixels. The fidelity
reference for both executors is the original runtime: a GPU picture is
qualified by its approximate agreement with that runtime, measured per mode,
exactly as a CPU picture is. VM/RNG state, draw placement, draw order,
lifecycle, savestate and rewind, actual-byte resource preservation and CPU
fast/general equivalence are exact in both modes and remain mandatory in
routine checks. Pixel queries in GPU mode read the image actually drawn; they
feed program logic, so a change to what they return is checked against the
runtime state the same route reaches in CPU mode. Exact placement means vertex
positions, extent, clipping and transform. The device fills each placed draw
with its own rasterization, sampling and texture filtering; that fill is picture
and is judged against the original runner. Never split a scaled, rotated or
filtered draw into CPU-shaped coverage rows or spans just to match CPU pixels.

Modern packaged nearest rotations with RGB-only writes on alpha-preserving numbered
surfaces reuse the CPU's vertex and source-bound preparation and submit one native
quad. The device samples its physical atlas coordinates and uses fixed source-over;
the alpha channel remains disabled. This bounded owner requires an ordinary program,
no GUI or explicit alpha test, source axes at most 4096, atlas axes at most 16384 and
placed coordinates within 65536. Other states retain their existing owners. Neutral
resource controls cover reflected placement and one-quad authority; backend controls
cover native UVs, cardinal angles, compatible batching and prewrite refusal. These
are contract checks; picture qualification still requires the original runner.

Modern complete one-texel sprites may magnify each axis up to 16384 through
an ordinary physical filtered quad, including replacement and fixed destination
factors. The source must have no trim, authored-edge projection or logical
margin. Placement, clipping, blend state and CPU execution retain their existing
owners. This extension never expands the draw into coverage rows. Neutral
resource controls require one packet, untouched CPU shadows and no observation;
two-texel replacement and excessive-scale replacement retain their CPU boundaries.
The OpenGL executor separately admits the same one-texel quad for normal,
zero-alpha replacement and inverse-destination factors. Its GL/GLES contract
controls require delivery to the driver and atomic refusal for nonunit crops
or scale beyond 16384; producer-only mocks do not establish executor admission.
Original-runner picture qualification remains separate from these controls.

Physical filtered font cells may retain a selected program when the existing
metadata predicate proves that the CPU leaves their source unchanged. They use
the same single placed glyph quad as an unselected program, without resetting
readable program state. Recognized or applicable programs retain their existing
owners. Neutral controls cover unit and magnified glyphs in world/GUI SCREEN
and numbered targets, with unchanged CPU shadows and no ordinary observation;
they establish admission, not original-picture qualification.

The CPU/OpenGL image comparisons in this repository are diagnostic. Routine
checks skip them; `make check GPU_PIXEL_PARITY=1` runs them and direct
execution uses `ANYGM_TEST_GPU_PIXEL_PARITY=1`. Real-device paired pixel probes
are separate explicit runs. The EXACT and TOLERANCE classes in the rows below
state what those controls expect when run: they locate where the two executors
differ and by how much, and they do not decide whether a GPU picture is
acceptable. A skipped comparison confers no qualification and does not change
its expected pixels or thresholds.

A full initial clear in a host-framebuffer plan may invalidate the obsolete colour attachment
before writing the new frame. This avoids retaining a larger frontend allocation's previous
contents on tiled devices. Partial plans and readback targets keep their previous contents;
unsupported drivers keep the existing path. An available buffer-clear API initializes only the
named host colour attachment after discard, including its undefined padding, so a tiled driver
can use a whole-attachment fast clear. Published pixels and later draw placement are unchanged;
default framebuffer zero retains its scoped clear. Maximum geometry, completed-frame bytes and state
transport are unchanged. The hint is optional in GLES 3 and desktop GL 4.3 or newer.

First-generation sampled rotated regions and modern numbered nearest sprites use constant
specializations of their existing sampling and alpha-lookup bodies. Region origin, alpha table
and the modern point-map samplers belong to the selected program, and each program owns its
derived lookup cache. Texel variants retain their program. Packet geometry, sampling equations,
snapshots, draw order and CPU rendering are unchanged; no new coverage pieces are introduced.
Ordinary integer-round colour, integer replacement and nearest-surface sampling also use
constant specializations of their unchanged bodies. Their three lazy programs are derived
context resources; validation, blending, maps, source palettes and CPU rendering stay intact.

Modern numbered nearest sprites may use the existing native colour body with nearest
texture filtering and separate fixed source-over blending, without predecessor copies or
inverse point maps. This executor leaf requires a physical affine map consistent with the
immutable atlas crop and snapped placement, uniform tint, positive draw opacity, RGB-only
or complete RGBA writes, and no margin, cutout, key, palette or row span. Other packets keep
their existing owner. The nearest selector reuses the clamped native program and physical
UV/float-alpha packing without changing the source filter, placed vertices or CPU rendering.
Device fill remains picture and must be checked against the original.

Native filtered glyph batches with no logical margins, source key or palette use a constant
plain-source specialization of the same shader body. The backend verifies every queued quad's
flat margin before selecting it. Other batches retain their existing programs; source colour,
alpha testing, filtering, geometry, blending and draw order are unchanged. The two additional
programs are derived context resources, never state bytes.

Resident alpha eligibility reuses a bounded four-way cache for immutable encoded atlas
windows, including detached glyph selections. Identity, source version, encoded backing,
allocation, crop and dimensions participate in the key. Mutable fonts and unclassified or
foreign atlas views retain the existing scan. The cached facts and work counter are derived
metadata, not serialized state or drawing algorithms.

Complete untrimmed filtered stretched cells use the existing native bilinear sampler on
their placed quad and physical UVs. The device supplies sampling and fill; the original
runner is the picture reference. Logical transparent-margin variants keep their existing
owner. Neutral driver controls retain placed vertices, raw cutout and opaque alpha stamping;
CPU drawing and admission remain unchanged.

Certified opaque-policy application-to-screen composition at identical source and placed
extents uses the existing raw framebuffer copy when its source was drawn on the device.
Clipping selects the corresponding source rectangle, and all stored RGBA channels are
preserved. Scaled, blended and CPU-uploaded sources retain the composition shader.

Automatic composition uses the same program-policy predicate as ordinary
surface draws. An unknown selected program may keep its CPU passthrough
owner only while a content-shader device is neither requested nor present;
the readable program ID is never rewritten. Recognized software evaluators,
applicable external executors,invalid selections and requested/available devices keep
their explicit refusal. Existing early nearest,independent vertical-plane,
CPU-current sample-plane and Studio automatic packets retain their geometry,
alpha and authority rules. CPU raster bodies and shader kernels are unchanged.
`resident_automatic_underlay_raster_camera` requires25 program states across
policies,cameras,source authority and filtering. The existing public surface
order,Classic CPU sample-plane and two-plane composition controls must also
pass with a disabled unknown selection:EXACT RGBA0 endpoints,individual2,
exact coverage/order,unchanged program state,zero ordinary observations and
exact actual-byte seals. Existing snapshot costs stay separately counted;
this program-policy repair does not claim whole-function or game closure.

R03-SPR-SOURCE-PALETTE carries the recognized threshold/indexed ten-colour
mapping by value in existing nearest textured device-checkpoint states. The existing
surface snapshot/evaluator resolves finite byte thresholds and colours;no
content GLSL or CPU source-image remapping is introduced. The ordinary GPU
sampler maps RGB before tint/blend,preserving raw alpha and the selected
geometry owner. Palette differences cut compatible batches;primitive
boundaries revoke the sampler state. CPU pixel bodies and canonical state
remain unchanged;the new transient state explicitly refuses CPU replay and
is observed as actual pixels at existing state/context boundaries.
Paired public sprite controls must retain EXACT RGBA0 endpoints,individual2,
exact coverage/disabled channels,zero ordinary transport/new snapshots and
detached actual-byte/state checks. Recognition,program bindings,unsupported
geometry and measured numerical boundaries remain explicit debt;this is
not complete function,game or performance qualification.
Filtered source palettes remain counted CPU debt:their sampled-byte mapping
and reconstruction need a separate qualified port. The ordinary nearest
modern-numbered exact predecessor is retained and counted,not introduced or
claimed snapshot-free by this mapping extension.

R03-SPR-ABSENT-ROTATION is EXACT RGBA0: an ordinary packaged rotated crop
wholly outside an already loaded physical atlas has no CPU alpha bounds and
writes nothing. The shared outside predicate preserves the resident prefix
before masked/fog setup across Classic/Studio, SCREEN/application/numbered
targets, GUI/world mapping, nearest/filtering, ordinary blends, masks, cutout,
signed scales, tint and opacity. It emits no draw and earns no visible-writer
credit. CPU raster bodies and shaders are unchanged. The paired
`atlas-absent-rotation` companion requires 672 unchanged CPU/device images,
exact coverage and detached actual bytes, zero ordinary transports and a
rejecting alpha-one negative. `resident_absent_rotated_sprite_boundaries`
requires near-axis turns, nonfinite inputs, intersecting crops, cached
positive alpha, active programs and enabled destination-colour factors to
retain their old owner. Runtime/special sources and phase/authority/deferred
boundaries remain debt. This does not qualify visible clamped rotations or
the axis copy/filter policies of an absent crop.

R03-AUTOMATIC-RASTER-CAMERA retains automatic composition in SCREEN pixels,
independently of the current world/GUI camera. Its unchanged CPU owner adds
the camera before subtracting it in the surface leaf. Ordinary stretched and
zero-turn surface suffixes reuse that prefix without requiring a zero camera;
non-GUI and other unqualified source/state owners remain explicit debt.
The neutral raster-camera authority matrix and the existing surface-order
offset-camera companion must pass: cheap endpoints EXACT RGBA0,individual
partial draws TOLERANCE+/-2,exact outside coverage/order,unchanged readable
camera,zero ordinary observations and exact actual detached bytes. This adds
no shader,float emulator,CPU writer or whole-function/game qualification.

Conditional SCREEN recovery carries the fixed alpha-test byte reference0..255
through the existing RGB-reduction predicate and point-copy packet. Both RGB
and opaque-written-alpha passes discard sampled alpha at or below the enabled
reference. The global empty-RGB predicate still ignores alpha. Unit/nearest
magnification,identity filtering,opaque-certified filtered magnification and
the all-discard255 endpoint retain their CPU geometry. Positive cutout on an
uncertified partial source with true two-axis filtered magnification remains
an explicit prewrite refusal: its CPU owner uses centred bilinear sampling.
The existing conditional recovery generator requires1008 correlated controls
for each cutout and point-cutout profile,covering all256 references in both
Studio policies. Each has464 EXACT RGBA0 and544 TOLERANCE+/-2 controls; unchanged
identity neighbours remain64. Alpha-one,outside-one and cutoff-tie negatives
must reject. Require stale shadows,positive device work,zero ordinary reads/
queries/copies/replay and exact actual bytes with the context detached. This
is bounded recovery qualification,not a whole API,game or speed claim.

R03-CLASSIC-RASTER-UNDERLAY keeps automatic raster composition independent of

independent vertical-plane and later CPU-current sample-plane owners. Public
GML surface draws retain their projection/fog rejection; no3D raster or CPU
algorithm changes. Sources,geometry,blend/mask/cutout,authority and alias fences
otherwise remain unchanged. Copies/interior/alpha/outside require EXACT RGBA0;
existing individual blend/fringe TOLERANCE+/-2 and ordered-composition4 remain.

The existing automatic-underlay generators additionally require4212 raster-state
vectors:3456 early resident,720 later CPU-current planes and36 independent

boundaries remain; the sample-plane720 are EXACT0,while only the existing
blend/fringe controls permit2. Persistent perspective/depth/culling/lighting/
fog state and nonidentity world scale must survive each copy unchanged.
Require exact outside coverage,zero admitted ordinary observers/copies/replay,
actual-byte seals/detachment,the existing pixel negatives and unchanged public
surface/underlay neighbours. These are not game,speed or full-API qualification.

R03-CLASSIC-SAMPLE-UNDERLAY removes a cold SCREEN observation when later-Classic
automatic presentation already has CPU-current native or doubled filtered sample
planes. It copies those existing planes on the GPU; it never computes them on the
CPU for a device-owned root. The established resident phase owner stays first.
Known resident SCREEN ownership survives GUI admission until a real CPU writer.
The public automatic-underlay generator's cpu-sample-planes profile requires720
EXACT RGBA0 pairs across both later policies,owned/borrowed sources,world/mapped
GUI,native nearest/native filtered/doubled filtered,three alpha classes,two sizes
and five clipped/empty placements. It requires432 painted and288 empty pairs,
zero ordinary reads/masks/queries/snapshots/replay,unchanged source and stale
destination shadows,and24 exact actual-context-detached seals. Three pixel
negatives must reject. Unqualified shader/blend/mask/cutout state,source-lease,device-only source,alias and
missing-plane refusals remain explicit CPU debt; this is not whole-function,
all-frame,all-variant,game-speed or zero-upload qualification. Existing early
automatic-underlay and independent-root/numbered controls remain unchanged.

The existing public classic-step-composition generator's inactive-interpolated
profile requires1296 complete pairs across three policies,replaced bindings,
world/GUI,twelve public ports and Classic/application/both plane groups:

R03-SPR-INDEPENDENT-SUFFIX preserves the actual persistent image when a
full-sprite call takes an already unadmitted software suffix after an
independent device clear. The shared unmasked entry takes its checked CPU
barrier after ordinary no-write predicates. The shared masked preparator
takes that barrier before the unchanged CPU wrapper snapshots preserved
channels. Neither path admits the unsupported parent or changes CPU raster
arithmetic. A fresh pending clear resolves directly on CPU with zero device
submits or reads; an already published device prefix is observed only when
the real software suffix needs it. EXACT RGBA0 is a handoff property with
identical inputs,not new GPU draw or whole-function coverage credit.
The resource generator checks3984 correlated cases across six policies,
application/numbered owners,pending/published clears,legacy/on-demand
observation,all16 masks,normal/additive,tint,alpha,rotation/reflection and
three unmasked empty-call neighbours. Native retained,base-writer and
doubled-interpolated actual-host fixtures own delivered pixels and same-load
state/rewind proof. The last still has CPU drawing debt; repairing its
authority is not admitting its auxiliary sample planes. Mask-wrapper
preparation on empty masked calls retains its existing ordered CPU boundary.

R03-CLASSIC-STEP-COMPOSITION keeps a retained root sample resource independent
of a positive,explicitly stacked,non-base numbered target even after the active
CPU vertical pointer is detached. The existing real/sprite axis and rotated
font,selected-background and selected-tile owners retain their ordinary packets;
no source sampling,CPU arithmetic or root-plane algorithm changes. The existing
public-family generator's detached-vertical companion requires432 complete

and version,exact no-write coverage,positive family work,zero ordinary
reads/masks/queries/snapshots/replay and actual-byte recovery with EGL detached.
Its three existing pixel negatives must reject. The retained-vertical432 and

and unstacked bindings retain their old fences;this is bounded ownership,not
new SCREEN/application-zero,whole-function,game or performance qualification.

R03-SPR-COLLAPSED-ADDITIVE is a bounded empty-hardware-footprint policy,
not an inverse-coordinate overflow emulator. Packaged nearest additive sprites
with each signed scale magnitude2^-52..2^-49,integer mapped camera-relative
anchors and certified opaque destinations may retain the queued prefix when
every immutable crop-border RGB byte is at most2. Source/trim/origin,finite
state and ordinary owner limits remain explicit. No CPU pixel writer,shader,
destination observation or source upload is introduced. Black borders retain
EXACT RGBA0;the low-contribution exception allows individual RGB2 with exact
alpha,outside and disabled channels. Coloured borders,unknown/nonopaque alpha,
filtering,program/fog/cutout,other blends and all other scales keep their existing
owners or CPU debt. The shared public collapsed-additive profile must pair all
six policies,four owners,world/GUI and six representative states,including
independently coloured-border CPU boundaries,three pixel negatives,zero ordinary
transport and exact detached actual bytes. Empty culls have no visible GPU draw
credit. This is not whole-function,game,portable CPU-overflow or speed clearance.

R03-SPR-LARGE-AXIS-PACKET extends the existing nonpreserving unrotated
SCREEN/application row owner to Studio policies,mapped GUI,signed axes,
raw cutout and masks. Shared inverse rows and immutable sampler rectangles
are intersected with the existing CPU packet visitor's vector/scalar coverage.
Hardware nearest and fixed source-over own colour and alpha;one compatible
batched alpha pass stamps scalar tails. No destination snapshot,new CPU
raster,CPU replay or float-emulation recipe is introduced. The2048-quad,
8192-coverage-region and existing per-pixel visitor limits remain bounded.
Opaque/binary endpoints retain EXACT RGBA0,individual blends allow2 and
outside/disabled channels stay EXACT0. Preserving owners,Classic,other blend
states and filtering retain their existing executors or explicit debt.
First-generation footprints of262144 or more padded visible pixels use the
separate byte-alpha/cull owner only when the unchanged sample-row visitor
positively marks that coverage choice. Its existing CPU UV consumer and
INTEGER_ROW_TINT_TRUNC packet retain nearest sampling,fixed source-over,
raw input-alpha cull and covered-alpha stamping,including unknown/partial
target alpha. No CPU colour or destination bytes enter this preparation.
A larger framebuffer alone does not select that leaf:the unchanged alpha16
coverage visitor still proves smaller footprints first. At those extents,
cull remains readable and ignored by the CPU.
The existing integer endpoint owner remains available after proof refusal.
Unmarked or over-budget rows retain their previous owner or counted CPU debt.
Atomic capacity refusal rolls back only the incoming group,delivers its
validated queued prefix once and retries with the advanced image version.
It does not split a sprite or add ordinary transport;compatible groups
continue batching up to the existing capacity.

Require exact detached actual-byte sealing and rejecting alpha-one/outside-one/

R03-ATLAS-CLAMPED-WINDOW preserves a logical source rectangle larger than its
immutable physical atlas without creating replacement artwork. General filtered
taps clamp to physical storage; raw logical padding stays transparent. Canonical
aligned white copies instead retain the CPU cache's zero extension,clipped
geometry and exact texel-centre fetches. CPU-default bodies remain unchanged.
Absent-source raw CPU specializations cannot supply a deterministic pixel oracle;
their metadata-only refusal controls are reference debt,not GPU qualification.

R03-VERTEX-SPANS admits bounded visible untextured temporary2D vertex buffers
through the existing backend seam. CPU-identical coverage prepares ordered rows;

ordinary readback or exact CPU replay. Flat opaque endpoints require EXACT
RGBA0; partial-alpha draws allow2,with independent EXACT outside coverage.
Points,independent/strip lines and list/strip/fan triangles share one public
paired generator across six policies and six SCREEN/application/numbered/GUI
owners. The20736 state vectors and3672 bounds/topology vectors include explicit
CPU boundaries and no-credit no-ops,2049-point batching and432 detached states.
Opaque RGB gradients except independent points,textures,active3D,non-passthrough programs,
mixed-zero/tiny alpha,uncertain repeated fragments and over-budget geometry
remain counted CPU debt. This is not full vertex/model or game qualification.

Dense source-free coverage keeps its small8192-row allocation and grows only

and device preflight admit that larger count only for typed vertex rows,not
ordinary flat primitives. Whole-batch validation still precedes every write;

Source-free rows use the same CPU-passthrough program gate as textured rows.
This preserves the CPU draw's meaning,not a claim that authored GLSL has no
effect. Readable program selection and metadata stay unchanged; no content
program is compiled by this admission. The public `vertex-visible` programs
profile pairs20736 correlated controls across six policies/owners/ports and
the existing blend/mask/transform matrix. Invalid selections,recognized fragment
or vertex-colour families and applicable external executors remain exact CPU
boundaries. This does not broaden textured,depth,precision or source budgets.

R03-VERTEX-TEXTURED extends that same coverage preparation to versioned atlas
and non-aliased CPU/device-current surface sources. Nearest rows carry selected
texel centres for EXACT RGBA0 endpoints. Filtered rows use hardware repeat and
bilinear colour/factors within individual tolerance2. Integer repeat periods
are removed before device conversion. Studio UV bands stay within half a
period; Classic fixed-function eight-bit coordinate phases are prepared as
geometry,not source pixels. Both use the same bounded row budget. A separate raw-alpha
footprint preserves zero-alpha admission, including Classic integer coverage;
it does not emulate CPU floating-point colour arithmetic. GPU-origin surfaces
retain logical orientation and sampler wrap is restored after submission.
Recognized colour programs, active3D, feedback, unsafe geometry/alpha and
over-budget or uncertain repeated fragments remain explicit drawing debt.
Mutable atlas writers flush before relocation/version changes; pending rows
also cut on source identity/version,not just pointer/extent. Only changed input
versions upload again. This is separate from ordinary GPU-to-CPU observations.

Optional scene mode discards a whole temporary2D vertex batch only when every
selected finite transformed vertex lies beyond one common viewport plane with
a two-pixel margin. R03-VERTEX-EMPTY preserves pending work,source/target
authority and derived triangle depth preparation without observing a texture,
closing a resident pass or inventing GPU draw credit. CPU-default bodies stay
unchanged. Active3D and unqualified visible/state combinations keep the CPU
executor; the bounded visible counterpart is R03-VERTEX-SPANS.
The public paired `vertex-empty` generator requires4320 EXACT RGBA0 controls,
900 visible-or-CPU witnesses,180 actual detached images and three rejecting negatives.
This geometric no-op rule is not whole-function or game qualification.

The multiview coordinator's full owned-application copy uses the coarse
`gml_render_multiview_copy_to_owned_application` facade. The renderer alone
resolves its owned pointer and extent before calling the unchanged raw copier;
this repairs the coordinator's storage boundary without acquiring CPU pixels or
altering composition. The existing435 R03-MULTIVIEW-COMPOSITION pairs exercise
this facade as well as the other raw-copy ports;old negatives and state controls
remain unchanged. The CPU coordinator's composition body is not modified.

Promoting a forced world from the CPU logical root to an equally sized owned
application preserves the CPU mask-before-copy order: R03-PROMOTED-WORLD-MASK
applies the same bounded opaque CLEAR bands to that promoted image as well as
the independent external root. Explicit application canvases which already
owned the CPU world retain root-only masking. CPU raster and mask bodies stay
unchanged. The new mask is EXACT RGBA0, ignores draw blend/alpha/cutout/masks,
does not change current bindings, and uses no ordinary read, snapshot or replay.
Its typed port refuses GUI, numbered/stacked targets and extent mismatch. Without
an owning-current witness it uses checked canonical observation and the existing
CPU flat rectangle executor, counted as ordinary CPU work, not GPU credit. A failed
observation remains a failure; witnessed normal play retains device-only bytes.
The base-history `owned-mask` companion compares144 complete images against the
unchanged CPU mask and144 sealed context-detached state roundtrips; alpha-one
and inside-one negatives must reject. The exported forced-narrow-overdraw
fixture exposes the engine ordering which a GUI clear hid in older fixtures.

External logical-root masking is EXACT RGBA0 and does not select a draw target.
`gml_render_completed_screen_mask` submits at most four disjoint opaque-black
CLEAR bands to the actual borrowed allocation, with a current owning witness,
an admitted resident pass and scene mode enabled. Owned/numbered aliases and a third unobserved external
allocation refuse. Existing raw-linear history retains each allocation's tail
through pitch changes; explicit CPU consumers, state replacement and controlled
destruction observe actual bytes. Ordinary accepted masking does not read pixels,
snapshot a destination or retain CPU replay. The current target, stack, camera,
GUI and pending-operation order remain unchanged. Device comparison
R03-EXTERNAL-ROOT-MASK (`base-history`, profile `mask`) pairs the unchanged CPU
mask with six policies, four active owners, world/GUI, clipped/empty/interior
regions, pitched history and exact sealed detached bytes. This is not whole-game
or speed qualification. Necessary initial target upload remains a separate cost.

Explicit positive numbered targets may open an independent resident child before
the first world pass, but only with scene mode enabled and a successful owning
context witness. `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_surface_set_target` leaves the
global scene closed, saves a CPU parent's deferred writes and restores its exact
pass/camera/extent; nested Studio and replacing Classic rules are unchanged.
SCREEN, GUI and application zero are not implicitly admitted. Each drawing family
retains its existing EXACT RGBA0 or individual TOLERANCE±2 contract and refusals.
`test_render_resources.c::independent_numbered_target_admission` covers missing,
false and disabled witnesses, packaged/runtime sources, grouping, nesting and CPU
aliases. The `surface-function-matrix` independent profile supplies144 real RGBA
pairs, stale-shadow/ordinary-transport checks and exact sealed detached bytes.
This lifecycle scope is not whole-game, timing or whole-drawing-API qualification.

The neutral operation IDs and families live only in
`src/video/renderer/gml_render_operations.def`. Private CPU and GPU ledgers are
`cpu/gml_render_cpu_operations.def` and `gpu/gml_render_gpu_operations.def`.
`tests/architecture/check_render_operations.py` checks one classification per ID,
real definitions for supported/fallback anchors, naming of the eight required
OpenGL counterparts, absence of fake definitions for planned entries, and the
live repair-level map's required fields, ledger status and source anchors.
`--require-delivered` rejects any planned entry; it is a final-delivery gate,
not the P1 CPU milestone. A planned entry cannot be selected. The historical
P1 CPU-only classification is retained below only as a dated architectural
snapshot; the live ledger and rule table here describe current support.

## Experimental scene fidelity contract

Ordinary scene submission packs each quad's existing four corner positions,
UVs, colours and shared sampling operands into52 floats rather than six expanded
28-float vertices. An instanced vertex shader reconstructs the identical triangle
order; fragment programs, CPU writers and the EXACT/TOLERANCE classification do
not change. The compact stream appends to a fixed four-batch-capacity buffer;
only exhaustion or context recreation allocates/orphans storage. Attribute byte
offsets include the current region, and an alpha-stamp pass reuses that region.
There are no new fences, waits, readbacks or admissions. Exact-program submissions
retain their separately qualified expanded layout and upload path. The neutral
`scene_resident_batch` driver control covers cross-submit offsets, wrap and context
loss; existing real-device paired scene controls retain responsibility for pixels.

The same stream keeps at most128 byte-identical range identities and an optional
CPU mirror bounded by that fixed8.125MiB buffer capacity. A word fingerprint only rejects
candidates: full byte equality is required before reusing an uploaded range.
Draws and all source/target/program/blend/attribute state still execute normally;
this caches vertex bytes, not an image, draw or source version. Cache identities
are discarded on orphan, context replacement and reported draw error. Allocation
failure leaves the established append-only upload path available. No shader,
admission, CPU writer, fidelity class or state format changes. The neutral
`scene_instance_reuse` control checks actual fetched VBO bytes on both APIs,
modified first/last instances, ring wrap, bounded-index eviction, draw failure and
context recreation; paired real-device controls still own pixel qualification.

Internal linear-history band copies and resident resize copies carry the SURFACE
family through their ordinary typed packets. They remain nearest1:1 raw storage
copies; family accounting must not report those accepted copies as unclassified.
Geometry, executor selection and RGBA copying retain their existing controls.

Known revalidation debt: the inherited modern SCREEN point control rejects its
alternating-tint partial-alpha case at scale0.7 at the admission/batch-mechanism
stage on both the frozen pre-change producer and the queue experiment. Pixels
are not reached. This is not a passing comparison or a newly attributed pixel
defect; the old draw-count and RGBA assertions remain unchanged.

Scene image transfers remain EXACT RGBA0. Uploads from64 KiB through16 MiB use
one context-local pixel-unpack buffer, orphaned before each mapped write and
unmapped before texture submission. This avoids a slow direct-driver copy without
changing conversion, row origin, stride, image versions or immutable-page reuse.
Smaller and over-budget transfers retain the direct path. Failed allocation,
mapping, unmapping or submission publishes no valid uploaded version; context
loss forgets the buffer without deleting into its successor. The existing
mapping entry-point adoption contract is unchanged. No gameplay readback,
completion barrier, new shader cache or drawing variant is introduced.
`scene_staged_upload` covers conversion, reuse, refusal and lifecycle; real-device
full-byte controls and the existing paired scenes establish pixels, not the fake driver.

R03-SPR-FILTERED-EDGE-NOOP retains fully clipped ordinary first-generation
filtered axis sprites on application targets without draining their GPU prefix.
It reuses the existing projected rectangle: authored-edge pages and integral
scales also agree on other targets. Fractional nonapplication coverage and all
other sampling/state exclusions remain explicit debt. No CPU raster body,
visible GPU sampling, shader, arithmetic recipe or transport is changed.
The `axis-turn-edges` selector reuses the complete ordinary function generator
at all four target edges and requires exact CPU axis classification, D2 RGBA,
independent coverage and unchanged transport; rejection is not GPU coverage.

R03-SPR-AXIS-TURN uses the existing CPU near-zero phase classification for
interpolation-enabled Studio resident sprites, including signed full turns.
Existing first-generation masked filtered variants have unqualified axis-turn
debt: zero/full-turn admission can differ, and an attempted admission-only
normalisation exposes an unchanged zero-angle cutout/disabled-blend GUI case
outside D2. The existing public assertions are retained; these are not newly
qualified visible variants of R03-SPR-ROUNDED-EMPTY-AXIS. Their renderer bodies
remain unchanged until a separately bounded repair qualifies the whole slice.
Their ordinary GPU preferences share that classification. Existing nearest
owners already resolve the phase and retain their qualified packet/batching preference.
Only the GPU admission
argument becomes zero; CPU raster bodies, authored caller values and draw state
stay unchanged. Existing zero-angle owners retain their sampling, blending,
target and state limits. The same selector pairs the modern automatic application
target's opaque screen-stage sprite semantics: its already offered hardware
bilinear packets now share the SCREEN validator's fixed blending/coverage path.
Numbered targets, alpha-preserving stages and application-target TEXT remain
excluded from that extension. The `axis-turn` paired selector checks admission and
explicit rejection equivalence across all ordinary policies/targets/GUI states,
complete D2 RGBA, independent coverage and unchanged ordinary transports.
First-generation filtered numbered turns remain explicitly rejected: an uncovered
zero-angle partial-alpha crop has measured legacy D2 debt and is not extended.
It does not convert a rejected zero-angle variant into GPU coverage or introduce
a shader, float-emulation recipe, destination snapshot or replay mechanism.

R03-P7-INDIRECT-GUI-HOST-NEAREST preserves the two separate nearest stages of
writer-free indirect GUI and host magnification. It composes their existing integer
axis maps rather than replacing them with one direct scale. Complete delivered
RGBA is EXACT0, including opaque alpha and black margins; the logical zero-high-byte
picture remains a deferred recipe and is observed only when needed. No new shader,
pixel upload/read, destination copy or persistent texture is added by composition.
Both GUI-to-logical and logical-to-host axes must enlarge or remain identical.
Reduction/mixed axes, CRT, crops, centered/excluded GUI and CPU-written sources
retain their existing owners. These controls do not establish game or speed qualification.

R03-PRESENT-INTEGER-SHIFT composes an already-qualified completed host picture
with the unchanged delivery-only CPU translation semantics. Earlier host/crop/GUI
rows describe their base stage; their former shift exclusion is superseded only
when an owning-current witness and the complete immutable source are available.
Direct presentation reuses its existing unshifted completed cache. Host fitting
uses a single-use transient device-only raster,never canonical state. Neither
path shifts saved pixels or adds an ordinary CPU read or a new direct snapshot.
Opaque black bands and RGB are exact; frontend XRGB alpha is independently255.
CRT/reduction/other transforms still require their separately qualified base path.

R03-P7-INDIRECT-GUI completes a pending automatic application underlay in the
existing resident SCREEN before its terminal scale, even when GUI code wrote
nothing. Recording another terminal application presentation at that point would
force both stages through CPU materialization. The compositor, scale conventions,
CPU branch and unsupported-state recovery are unchanged; this adds no shader,
destination snapshot or source sampling variant. The neutral automatic-empty
indirect fixture must preserve the existing strict SCREEN authority, transport,
transactional observation and exact state assertions, with real-device paired
RGBA, Reset and same-load state/rewind controls. Existing visible/direct GUI,
reduction and host-magnification neighbours remain required. Real-device controls
own raster proof; the graphics fake deliberately returns a diagnostic readback
pattern and cannot establish pixels for hardware-only source draws.

R03-P7-APPLICATION-HOST-NEAREST retains the automatic application's leading-edge
point selection before the existing host nearest magnifier. Its distinct composed
axis permits identity, enlargement, reduction and mixed inner axes; the host stage
must still enlarge or remain identical. It retains the full logical recipe for
explicit observation rather than making host pixels state authority. CPU presentation
bodies, sampling policy and source blend admission are unchanged. Complete typed
device controls require EXACT RGBA0, including ignored source-alpha bits, opaque
delivery, black margins and separately exact logical recovery. Partial coverage,
alias, interpolation, shader, CRT, crop/shift and host box reduction retain their
existing owners. This is a candidate until the listed focused controls qualify it;
it does not establish a whole game, frontend state transport or performance.

R03-RAW-LINEAR-BASE preserves borrowed SCREEN storage with EXACT RGBA0 when
the same allocation changes pitch. A separate device high-water image retains
the inactive suffix across shrink/grow; disjoint nearest1:1 bands map identical
linear indices, not a top-left rectangular intersection. Consecutive bands
share a complete-source clamp and the existing quad capacity. Both staging
images must finish before publishing the new owner; failed stages cannot write
the live CPU allocation. Newly exposed, never-resident host bytes may upload,
but ordinary migration never observes GPU pixels or retains CPU replay.
Explicit observation merges only the historical suffix before observing the
newest active prefix. State/context unbinding preserves the established
presentation-storage policy; genuine loss discards this volatile device history.
CPU-current bases without history and non-on-demand devices keep their existing
CPU path. No state fields, CPU resize body or presentation geometry are changed.
Device comparison R03-RAW-LINEAR-BASE uses the `base-history` selector.
Its180 small controls and180 multi-row controls require360 complete RGBA pairs;
logical application borrows also observe the suffix outside the active SCREEN.

R03-P5-SURFACE-RESIZE now copies device-only application or numbered storage
with EXACT RGBA0. `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::surface_resize_resident`
stages a transparent full clear and an unconditional nearest1:1 intersection
into a new incarnation before replacing any borrowed pointer. The existing
direct-texture shader copies partial and zero alpha without blending, tint,
alpha-test or camera state; there is no destination snapshot or pixel read.
Already-current CPU pixels retain the existing host copy without a new upload.
Legacy non-on-demand devices and numbered deferred clears keep their established
CPU lifecycle path. A failed stage preserves the old resource for ordered CPU
observation; a failed observation leaves the old allocation bound. This is
rectangular resize, not raw-linear SCREEN reinterpretation: the separate
R03-RAW-LINEAR-BASE rule owns borrowed-base tail preservation. The neutral
`resident_surface_resize_storage`, `resident_surface_resize_refusal` and
`resident_surface_resize_state` controls cover ownership/aliases/failure,
metadata-only state capacity, root application observation and exact numbered
save/load/save, while device pairs cover chained
growth, shrink and mixed extents, complete RGBA and detached sealed bytes.
No whole-route residency or speed claim follows from this lifecycle rule.

R03-SPR-INTEGER-FUNCTION extends ordinary packaged nearest sprites to both signs,
uniform tint, normal/disabled/inverse-source blending and integral physical scales across the
ordinary targets. Its paired matrix owns that extension. Older sprite
rows describe the limits of their cheaper retained recipes, not a global CPU
rejection of combinations separately qualified by this rule. Covered SCREEN or
application alpha needs at most one additional pass per compatible batch; it
never requires a predecessor snapshot or one draw per overlapping sprite.
The established modern numbered-target world nearest predecessor recipe remains
preferred after the cheaper existing integer crop refuses. This extension must
not shadow that separately guarded owner. Such unchanged predecessor controls
are counted explicitly in the paired generator and retain their documented
snapshot/batching debt; the zero-snapshot and eight-call grouping requirement
applies to the new route, not a qualification of that old recipe's replacement.
Classic's logical packaged sprite writer remains nearest when presentation
interpolation is enabled. Integer and bounded nearest full-sprite admission,
including masked dispatch, requires the same sprite-function-matrix with the
Classic interpolation selector across ordinary targets and states: EXACT RGBA0
endpoints or individual TOLERANCE±2, exact coverage and disabled channels.
The CPU flag and writer remain unchanged. Auxiliary phase planes, stored-extent
mismatches, special sources, other blends and the existing physical bounds retain
their explicit rejection; this does not qualify filtered Studio or whole games.
Magnified first-generation application sprites use this integer geometry owner
for fractional-origin partial alpha too: the retained float-phase preference
can lose trailing texel ties on larger cells. The paired late-text/large-sprite
control and complete first-generation sprite matrix guard that selection.
New fractional controls of that unchanged inherited recipe are explicit D8 debt,
not qualification of R03-SPR-INTEGER-FUNCTION. A D3D12 control with a negative
unit axis showed an EXACT-channel difference13; native verification remains
pending, so no driver-independent defect or tolerance waiver is inferred.
The generator's strict default rejects that difference. Its explicit debt mode
reports the old route's full-RGBA failures separately while retaining every
original D2 gate and strict D2 for all new/modified packets. Both the inherited
implementation and its numbered-world selection remain unchanged.
Raw-byte cutout discards alpha<=the reference before draw-alpha modulation;
all references0..255 are paired. Inverse RGB uses unattenuated integer-tinted
source colour, with zero/inverse-source-alpha blending on preserving targets.
Disabled inverse blending still replaces raw zero-alpha texels opaque unless
cutout discards them. Ordinary normal blending keeps its old zero-alpha discard.
Four disabled-blend controls use finite positive draw alpha1e-300, one per
normal/cutout/inverse/inverse-cutout state: exact source writes survive vertex
float underflow. Forty-eight exact ordered threshold/enable images qualify
observable batch splits; skipped-cutoff injection must fail. No ordinary reads,
destination snapshots or CPU float emulation are added by these states.
Inactive references are normalized only in executor snapshots, not readable
draw state. Ninety-six exact groups vary all256 inactive references in normal
and disabled inverse drawing: each stays in one driver draw without exhausting
the pending state's128-entry table, and the public reference remains255.

R03-TILE-CLASSIC-UNIT qualifies ordinary Classic positive-unit nearest tiles
on borrowed SCREEN and numbered targets. Integral contained packed source
selections and target coverage, opaque/binary alpha and white/full draw alpha
require EXACT RGBA0. The background-tile port keeps its shared source clipping;
single/room tile overruns remain unqualified. Empty selected/target coverage
preserves queued work. Owning on-demand authority, existing compatible nearest
batches and actual-pixel checkpoints add no shader, ordinary reader, destination
copy or CPU replay. Phase planes, filtering, tint, partial visible alpha, GUI/3D,
reflection and nonunit/fractional coverage retain debt. The classic-unit-tile
control pairs complete unchanged CPU/device RGBA and actual sealed no-current
bytes; whole-game fallback and speed qualification remain separate.

R03-TEXT-MAPPED-INTEGER extends the shared nearest crop only for opaque/binary
full-alpha black/white text at positive whole scales1..4. The text owner already resolved
world mapping and snapped glyph anchors. Every newly admitted span requires
on-demand actual-pixel checkpoint authority. Integral target clipping retains the
full UV phase; the variant adds
no ordinary observation or CPU replay. R03-SPR-INTEGER-ROUNDED-NOOP retains
positive whole nearest sprites whose half-up rounded physical crop is empty,
including subpixel slivers outside the raster. Integral cameras exclude the
modern half-camera tie. Both require EXACT RGBA0. The mapped-integer-text device
control pairs the unchanged CPU raster, queued prefixes and empty sprite calls;
partial-alpha text, reflections, filtering, rotation and special states receive
no new qualification. Whole-game speed remains a separate gate.

R03-TILE-INTEGER-CLIP extends the existing crop to positive whole nearest tile
scales1..8, including already-resolved world mapping and target-edge clipping.
Opaque/binary source alpha, white RGB and full drawing alpha require EXACT RGBA0.
The original full quad preserves UV phase while the viewport clips enlarged
texels. Clipped nonunit geometry requires owning on-demand observation and keeps
actual device pixels; it adds no CPU replay implementation or ordinary read.
Immutable nearest TILE checkpoints share the existing compatible atlas batch.
Integral anchors and projected axes are bounded to65536, atlas axes to16384;
trim, fractional source/geometry, reflection, filtering, rotation, partial alpha
and special states gain no new admission. The `integer-tile-clipping` control
requires9216 complete-RGBA CPU/device pairs, one compatible driver draw, stale
shadows, zero ordinary reads/masks/queries/copies/replay and exact explicitly
sealed actual-no-current bytes. One-alpha-level corruption must fail.
Existing576 unit-source and288 background comparisons remain unchanged.
This is not whole-game, frontend-state or speed qualification.

R03-TILE-UNIT-SOURCE-CLIP clips integral positive-unit nearest tile selections
against the untrimmed packed page using the existing CPU/shared part-view.
Right/bottom overrun and empty source/destination selections are EXACT RGBA0;
visible results reuse the ordinary nearest batch without a destination copy,
observer, shader change or CPU replay. Reflected/scaled, fractional-source,
trimmed, filtered, rotated and special-state extensions remain unqualified.
Exact nearest immutable TILE operations share the existing coalescible atlas
span with BACKGROUND operations; a family label alone no longer drains it.
Source/state/target/version and capacity guards remain unchanged.
The `unit-tile-source-clipping` paired control requires576 complete-RGBA cases,
one compatible prefix/subject/suffix driver draw, unchanged empty-subject target
authority/version, stale CPU shadows, zero ordinary reads/masks/queries/copies/replay
and exact explicitly sealed cached bytes after actual context detachment.
Whole-game fallback and speed qualification remain separate.

GPU scene binding at load, Reset and context recreation prepares the common scene,
automatic-composition and fixed font programs, geometry handles and destination-copy capacity at the current
engine extent through `gml_gpu_prepare_scene`. CPU scene selection does not call it.
Preparation requires accessible owning-context dispatch. Once per context, it executes the existing
terminal and automatic-composition pipelines into a private 1x1 target and waits during preparation, not gameplay: linking alone
need not finish driver compilation. The real frontend target is restored, never written or read;
the tiny upload and both draws are counted, without scene-execution credit or published image authority.
The composition writer reuses its ordered device-local predecessor copy, never a CPU observation.
Repeated bindings reuse capacity; context loss forgets it. Content programs and larger future extents
retain their existing demand-driven paths. Preparation failure is nonfatal and retains ordinary
lazy retry. Raster rules, EXACT/TOLERANCE classifications and state bytes are unchanged.

Room warming at initial start, Reset and room entry additionally prepares GPU copies of its
already decoded sprite/background pages. Packaged real/sprite-font pages are decoded/uploaded once
per distinct page at each boundary, within a 64 MiB aggregate speculative budget; the device
reuses existing immutable identity/version uploads. Fonts have priority; remaining budget also prepares
immutable pages whose first users are not yet live room instances. Mutable/runtime-rasterized pages, unsupported extents,
missing current-context proof and failures keep ordinary lazy paths. CPU warming is unchanged;
the sprite/background helpers do not upload mask-only pages solely for collision use. This is resource preparation, not
new draw admission, fallback exemption or zero first-use cost for every future content resource.

R03-P9-AUTOMATIC-UNDERLAY also prepares an already queued underlay before an
otherwise qualified public stretched application- or numbered-surface GUI draw with zero camera offset.
Qualified mapped SCREEN/GUI nearest real/sprite TEXT crops prepare that ordered prefix before
their unchanged atlas admission, as filtered glyphs already do. A witnessed
on-demand device also admits active cutout below255 for automatic first-generation
coverage or a certified opaque modern source. First-generation identity copies
still retain their raw source alpha; the unchanged CPU opaque fast leaf and
existing automatic shader both ignore that cutout. This is an admission extension,
not new arithmetic. Unknown/partial modern coverage, cutoff255, masks, shaders,
legacy ownership and existing geometry/source exclusions remain refused.
Nonzero cameras or active world transforms retain pending nearest-text CPU
fallback without first consuming the unqualified prefix.
The `nearest-text-underlay` control owns44 selected full-RGBA/order cases:
both font kinds, Studio13/15/17, cutoff endpoints, mapped/clipped and fractional
scales, plus first-generation partial-alpha identity/magnification. Opaque/binary
draws retain EXACT0 and the existing text matrix owns individual TOLERANCE±2;
the composed prefix/suffix limit is4 with exact independent far-region coverage.
Counters require no ordinary reads/masks/queries/replay, at most16 driver draws,
and exactly the compositor's existing one destination snapshot. Five explicit
admission exclusions, three pixel/order negatives, unchanged neighbours and
exact sealed actual bytes after context detach are required. No whole-game
speed or zero-read qualification is implied.
Changing automatic-draw enablement does not cancel that queued intent. The
existing compositor and its one device-local destination snapshot are unchanged;
the following surface uses its existing hardware sampling and fixed blend path.
No CPU leaf, shader arithmetic, observer or replay policy is added. Non-GUI,
nonzero-camera and the compositor's existing source/state exclusions retain CPU.
The paired `surface-underlay-order` control exercises both Studio policies,
CPU/device-current sources, GUI mapping, clipping and opaque/partial suffixes:
16 EXACT RGBA0 and16 TOLERANCE±2 controls,32 reversed-order negative controls,
zero ordinary reads/masks/queries/replay and exact sealed no-current pixels.
Its `numbered` profile adds576 correlated policy/source/GUI/alpha/clip/public-port/scale
controls:480 execute both operations on GPU, with160 EXACT RGBA0 and320 individual
TOLERANCE±2 cases (measured maximum1). The96 modern-Classic automatic-presentation
cases retain their existing CPU boundary and require exact reference pixels; they
are not GPU coverage. The `numbered-sampling` companion checks the same576 suffixes
without a pending prefix:192 EXACT0 and384 TOLERANCE±2, maximum1. The unchanged
CPU whole-source white/full-alpha normal magnification leaf selects leading-edge
point sampling even with interpolation enabled; numbered surfaces reuse the
existing nearest packet rather than the filtered owner for that leaf. Unit and
true partial/tinted filtered draws retain their previous owners. The existing
early-Classic compositor needs no destination snapshot; Studio still needs one,
and the suffix adds none. Independently authored solid CPU coverage, reversed
ordering, alpha/outside/tolerance/order negatives, unchanged surface-function
neighbours and exact actual-device sealing remain required. Camera/world,
special source/state and modern-Classic prefix policy remain explicit debt;
this does not qualify every public surface variant or a complete game.

R03-P5-GUI-FULLWIDTH extends first-generation GUI point presentation and corrected
1:1 partial-alpha composition with interpolation off/on.
It is EXACT for qualified integer opaque point geometry and TOLERANCE±2 for
individual noninteger geometry or partial alpha, with exact coverage in both categories.

R03-P5-GUI-OPAQUE-SUFFIX preserves SCREEN's known-opaque metadata after a
qualified opaque-source GUI point presentation or partial-alpha unit composition
whose rounded/clipped rectangle covers the whole destination. These existing
leaves write opaque alpha at every covered pixel. No pixels are inspected and
no sampling, blending or CPU raster implementation changes. Partial coverage,
nonunit partial-alpha and other states gain no new proof. This enables an
already-qualified partial-SCREEN box-filtered sprite suffix without a CPU barrier.
Its additional 240 authored controls require EXACT0 for opaque point prefixes
and outside-coverage bytes, TOLERANCE±2 for partial prefixes and identical-input
single suffix draws, and the independent composed±4 bound. Explicit test-only
prefix observation is excluded from the separately checked zero ordinary reads;
actual device bytes are sealed before exact no-current recovery.

Resident begin/loss causes are recorded only under the existing opt-in dispatch
diagnostics; see [resolved draw dispatch accounting](DIAGNOSTICS.md#resolved-draw-dispatch-accounting).
The per-public-frame census separates admission skips, begin predicates and the
first CPU execution that loses residency from draw volume. This metadata changes
no raster rule, EXACT/TOLERANCE classification or CPU reference behavior; a cause
count alone is not GPU execution or performance qualification.

The R03-P5-SURFACE-STRETCHED application-to-SCREEN candidate admits the
content-owned first-generation opaque GUI point-presentation leaf and ordinary
filtered magnified alpha/tint draws. Point coverage uses destination pixel
centres; hardware nearest copies RGB and writes opaque alpha. Qualified 1:1 or
integer point geometry remains EXACT; fractional point and hardware-bilinear
single draws require TOLERANCE±2. Both use fixed-function source-over and
versioned application sources, without predecessor copies or CPU replay.
Resource/backend call controls cover admission, clipping, refusal and capacity
batching. The first-generation GUI full-width extension below retains the CPU
source-aspect correction before point or partial-alpha unit preparation,
including centred width and width-capped height. Its paired complete-RGBA controls cover CPU-current and
device-rendered sources, GUI mapping, clipping, exact outside coverage and sealed
actual-no-current recovery. Other full-width states retain ordered CPU fallback;
whole-workload and speed qualification remain separate.
An admitted complete nearest replacement from a proven opaque atlas page at full
draw alpha restores the application-opacity metadata discarded by generic enqueue.
Partial coverage, binary/partial source alpha and partial draw alpha do not gain
that proof. This enables device-produced opaque application point presentation
without a source readback; CPU drawing bodies and expectations remain unchanged.

The identity SCREEN GUI extension of R03-SPR-COARSE-MIP, with interpolation
enabled or disabled as in the unchanged full-target CPU leaf, uses fixed-function
source-over, with EXACT zero/full-alpha endpoints and TOLERANCE±2 partial-alpha
single draws. Admission requires default fragment state,
full minified coverage and a positive owning-context on-demand witness. The
existing four-byte coarse-texel preparation/upload remains; the new GUI branch
needs no predecessor snapshot or CPU replay retention and preserves actual device
bytes only on observation. Unknown or nonopaque SCREEN destinations reuse the
hardware-sampled, batched alpha-only coverage pass: a positive full-target veil
writes opaque alpha as the unchanged CPU leaf does; a zero-alpha veil preserves
the destination. Known-opaque destinations keep their single-pass path.
Nonidentity GUI and unavailable witnesses retain the
ordered CPU path. `test_render_resources::resident_gui_coarse_veil_on_demand`
checks authority, witness refusal and explicit observation; the real-device
R03-SPR-COARSE-MIP GUI comparator checks RGBA, no ordinary reads/copies and exact
sealed recovery. The world branch and its exact comparison assertions remain
unchanged; no whole-game, all-GUI or speed qualification follows from this slice.

R03-SPR-GUI-MAPPED-STRETCH admits previously excluded centred/mapped SCREEN GUI
magnification of an untrimmed immutable atlas frame. Shared GUI mapping, camera
subtraction, floored origin and rounded extent match the unchanged CPU entry.
White/full drawing alpha, ordinary fragment state, positive enlargement on both
axes, a witnessed owning context and bounded finite geometry are required.
The existing hardware bilinear surface sampler clamps physical crop edges and
uses fixed source-over plus batched opaque-written-alpha coverage. Individual
draws are TOLERANCE±2; zero-alpha sources and outside coverage remain EXACT0.
There are no destination snapshots, CPU float-emulation shaders, ordinary reads
or retained replay recipes. Explicit observation seals actual GPU bytes before
no-current-context recovery. Existing identity/mapped-zero-camera exact paths,
trim/minification, tint, partial drawing alpha and special-state exclusions are
unchanged. Synthetic overlap controls prove batching, not accumulated fidelity
or whole-game speed.

Native filtered quads share a context-owned program compiled from the existing
filtered sampler branches alone. Other scene branches stay in the common program.
Quad placement and fixed blending are unchanged; sampler-specific colour and alpha rules follow below;
unused attributes are skipped and both programs follow context destruction/loss.
The native preset glyph control also verifies complete-quad admission and program reuse.
Complete first-generation filtered TEXT quads with ordinary source-over use the existing
continuous hardware colour kernel and a constant companion for opaque-written alpha. They
retain placement, source cutout and crop metadata without quantising sampled colour to CPU
bytes first. Other families, unit-point glyphs and prepared support packets retain their recipes.
The device's sampled result is picture; actual resource reads and restored bytes remain exact.
Native linear colour kernels omit their separate opaque-alpha pass only when the destination
has a context-local proof that every stored alpha byte is already 255. A full opaque clear or
complete raw copy from a proved source can establish it. Partial nonopaque clears, CPU uploads,
storage replacement, unknown writers and errors retire it. Ordinary source-over and disabled
alpha writes preserve existing opaque bytes; vector-alpha recipes remain conservative. The
proof is derived, never serialized, and changes no sampled colour or actual destination byte.
Unknown and partial-alpha destinations retain their original coverage pass. Neutral controls
cover both APIs, repeat submissions, partial clears, CPU replacement and full/partial raw copies.
Three further bounded programs compile the existing ordinary sampler with selector0 or14
constant, or selector77/78 restricted to the fixed-stretch pair. Their sampler, phase,
blend and predecessor arithmetic is retained verbatim; no CPU-shaped geometry is added.
The predecessor origin belongs to the selected program. All program objects share
the existing context loss/destruction boundary.

The GPU scene backend's CPU/GPU controls use EXACT for the qualified cheap exact
rules and TOLERANCE±2 for an individual filtered or partially blended draw with
identical inputs. A composed real-workload image is compared at TOLERANCE±4 per
eight-bit RGBA channel at every pixel. These classes measure agreement between
the two executors; acceptance of a GPU picture is decided against the original
runtime, as the opening section states. Placement (vertex positions, extent,
clipping and transform), draw order, runtime state and restoration of actual
saved GPU pixels are exact in every case. The device's rasterization, sampling
and filtering within the placed draw are picture, judged against the original;
CPU coverage comparisons below remain diagnostic rather than acceptance gates.
TOLERANCE±2 labels below describe individual draws, and rows reporting a
composed±2 result record a measurement, not a stricter class. Synthetic
repeated-surface accumulation is informative unless drift greater than sixteen
levels is demonstrated in a pattern used by an actual workload.

The same 864 physical controls on native NVIDIA Core OpenGL and llvmpipe
GLES/Core OpenGL preserve the exact clear/copy control and keep all 288 single
draws within one level in RGBA. Known synthetic accumulation maxima are
RGBA17/13/28/0 on native NVIDIA and RGBA32/21/32/0 on llvmpipe; the historical
composed±2 assertion fails 228 and 284 controls respectively. This is not solely
a D3D12 translation-layer effect. Four full-raster real-workload checkpoints on
each driver family remain within three RGB levels, meeting the composed±4 bound.
CPU frontend XRGB does not define alpha, so those checkpoints do not qualify
persistent-image alpha or every scene variant. Synthetic RGBA proof and exact
state restoration controls remain distinct from frontend-visible RGB evidence.

An additional full-output CRT workload comparison at 3440-by-1600 has one red
pixel five levels from CPU at its transition checkpoint on both native NVIDIA
and D3D12. Its other two checkpoints meet the four-level composed bound. This
known experimental deviation is not specific to the translation layer, does
not qualify the complete route, and does not change the per-pixel contract.

The empty-screen recovery operation reduces exact RGB-zero predicates to two
RGBA8 device flags, then conditionally draws the application image with the
existing leading-edge point geometry and fixed RGB blending. It neither assumes
a content shader drew nonblack pixels nor removes recovery when it did not.
Only plain Studio magnification on a witnessed resident SCREEN is admitted,
with either texture-interpolation setting: both CPU branches use the same
leading-edge point indices for magnification. Reductions do not share that rule;
classic, active-program, full-width, reduction and other state variants retain
the old query/recovery path. The device-only conditional image is observed for
state or controlled destruction, not retained as a CPU replay recipe.

Qualified magnified numbered-source composition is still experimental, not a
general claim of zero-transfer rendering. Unqualified geometry, source/state
variants, CPU content consumers and absent context-ownership proof retain their
established fallback. Boot-route read/mask/transport counters and single-pair
timings do not establish an ordinary-gameplay per-frame transfer budget. The
hardware additive descriptor remains backend-only; this contract does not itself
admit public additive sprite variants without workload qualification.

For checkpoint timing, `src/core/engine.c::anygm_run_frame` measures the final
`gml_render_scene_flush` as the existing profile's `seal` phase. The optional
adapter trace in `src/adapters/libretro/libretro_entry.c::retro_run` measures
the entire loaded frontend tick and callback envelopes, including that seal.
Its `retro_serialize_size`, `retro_serialize` and `retro_unserialize` boundaries
also report actual state-call results and CPU-visible costs through
`state_profile_begin` / `state_profile_report`, without changing state transport
or claiming frontend ring acceptance.
See [call-boundary profiling](LIBRETRO.md#optional-call-boundary-profiling) for
the exact clock, cadence and GPU-completion exclusions and neutral controls.
This instrumentation does not change any rule, shader or authority boundary
mapped below; checkpoint bytes remain product cost, not test overhead.

Retained SCREEN/application canonical checkpoints may use explicit-baseline
incremental reads (R03-P9-INCREMENTAL-CHECKPOINT). The backend compares current
RGBA8 pixels with the previous successful GPU checkpoint using exact texel
fetches, downloads a small 32-by-32 tile change mask and then one union rectangle
of changed pixels. The existing host row/channel converter packs that region;
the resource publishes only that rectangle after complete success. An unchanged
image publishes nothing. This preserves a complete current CPU-canonical frame
before frontend return; it does not eliminate all CPU data or observer cost.
Ordinary observers, cold/stale baselines, numbered targets, changed extents or
formats, unsupported detection, dense changes and targets above the independent
32-MiB GPU baseline limit retain full readback. Two baseline textures and one
small shared mask are context-local, bounded, disposable and never state bytes.
`readbacks`/`readback_bytes` count actual attempted pixel reads, including failed
ones; `checkpoint_masks`/`checkpoint_mask_bytes` separately count actual mask
reads. Logical checkpoint success is still resource-owned. Correctness controls
do not establish a whole-route transfer reduction or performance gain.

Completed-picture save preparation is owned by
`src/core/engine_state.c::state_write_completed_frame` and `::state_write`,
not a shader or raster executor. `::engine_state_save` permits an early stop
only after both existing encoding lower bounds exceed the supplied capacity;
the established frame-free writer then supplies the same bytes. A failed
compact attempt restores the original complete-plus-compact failure path.
CPU `::engine_state_size` and all fitting complete writes retain the full codec.
The comparative owner is
`tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::completed_frame_capacity_case`;
fixed/variable transport and non-current-context rewind are additionally
covered by the existing libretro state contracts. This CPU codec optimization
does not remove GPU checkpoints or change their authority/residency rule.

Active scene GPU size queries use `src/core/engine_state.c::state_size_is_metadata_only` and
`common/gml_render_state.c::gml_render_state_capacity`: metadata-only raw completed-frame and
inline-sprite ceilings, plus the existing surface RLE worst case. They neither observe device
pixels nor replay a frame or prepare pixel-compression caches. The actual save codec and
transactional rollback still consume exact canonical bytes, using the writer's payload length
rather than the allocation bound. CPU sizing remains exact. The controls are
`tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_size_query_is_metadata_only_case`,
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::metadata_state_capacity_preserves_pending_pixels`
and `::metadata_state_capacity_skips_pixel_compression`; the frontend observation-cost control
retains exact saved-byte, image/audio, fixed/variable ring and no-current-context checks.
This is a sizing prerequisite, not on-demand GPU preservation, volatile loss or removal of ordinary
replay/checkpoints. No raster rule or its EXACT/TOLERANCE classification changes.

The explicit state observer is `common/gml_render_resources.c::gml_render_resource_observe_device`,
using the existing staged checkpoint body without substituting a replay recipe.
`common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_observe_device_resources` drains pending work and
observes dirty numbered/application/owned SCREEN resources. The root writer calls it only after
`src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_graphics_is_current` receives the host's positive owning-thread
proof. An absent witness retains the existing resource writer. Negative installed proof rejects
unobserved images before CPU replay, using the metadata-only
`common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_device_observation_pending`; already observed CPU
images remain usable. Size queries never ask for that proof.
The adapter may resolve a thread-local platform getter through the frontend and compare the exact
adopted context identity at each observation; resolving GL entry points alone grants no access.
The controls are `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_state_observes_lent_device_case`,
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::device_observation_preserves_actual_replayable_pixels`
and `tests/contract/libretro_hardware_render.c::context_access_proof_tracks_exact_owner`.
They cover actual RGBA instead of a different synthetic replay response, failed-read publication,
unbound/foreign/replaced contexts and unchanged context-free state bytes. This does not certify
every frontend configuration.
Witnessed GPU scene services opt into `gml_gpu.c::scene_observe_on_demand` through
`gml_render_device.h::GmlRenderDeviceServices`. Successful resource submissions then publish
`GML_RENDER_RESOURCE_GPU_RESIDENT` without retaining operation journals, mutable source copies
or replay recipes, even for an EXACT operation. Consecutive draws and byte-free resident sources
keep actual device authority until a CPU/state observer; temporary observation failure preserves
that version for retry. Proven pre-write refusal observes an earlier resident prefix before
ordered CPU fallback; uncertain writes invalidate it instead of replaying stale CPU pixels.
Actual context loss invalidates unobserved resident resources without calling the dead device.
`src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_graphics_bind_renderer` enables this policy only for GPU scenes;
CPU scenes, absent witnesses and negative initial proofs retain their established journal policy.
The controls are `tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::resident_authority_avoids_replay_retention`,
`::resident_observation_retry_and_fallback`, `::resident_source_copy_and_loss` and the existing
engine state/loss controls below. EXACT/TOLERANCE raster classification and CPU kernels are unchanged.
Content CPU fallbacks still require observation; this is not a zero-read or performance claim.
`src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_graphics_finish_scene` uses
`gml_render.c::gml_render_scene_submit_pending` for witnessed current scene passes instead of
the all-resource checkpoint seal, including world, GUI, multiview and final frame boundaries.
Existing CPU-reader barriers remain; CPU scenes and absent-witness hosts keep the established
seal. `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_frame_end_defers_observation_case` keeps a
pending persistent image across three frames without a checkpoint/replay, then saves/restores
the actual GPU RGBA; its absent-witness control still seals each frame.
`tests/unit/video/gpu/test_gpu.c::unbound_context_access_case` checks desktop/embedded refusal
before upload, readback, submit, flush, wait, synchronous query and terminal-plan graphics calls,
with rebind/retry and initial-adoption refusal. Cached capabilities, program failures and logical
retirement remain metadata-only; ordinary GL work requires positive installed proof when present.
`tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_state_unbound_observation_is_retryable_case`
also preserves resource/frame authority and caller bytes across unavailable complete, frame-free
and rollback observations, then rebinds and restores actual RGBA.
`::scene_destroy_observation_fault_case` covers negative proof, one bounded transient retry and
persistent read failure. The latter reports loss and invalidates still-unobserved volatile pixels
rather than fabricating GPU bytes. Healthy controlled destruction preserves the actual pixels.

Completed-frame transport is EXACT for the actual GPU input RGBA, not a claim that a mixed scene
matches the CPU raster byte for byte. `src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_present_hardware_screen`
captures eligible scene output with positive context proof through
`gpu/gml_gpu.c::gml_gpu_capture_completed_frame`; `gpu/opengl/gml_render_gpu_opengl_presentation.c`
owns a separate snapshot texture/FBO, extent and generation, never the ordinary shader readback target.
Capture performs GPU-to-GPU copy only. `src/core/engine_presentation.c::engine_materialize_completed_frame`
reads this snapshot on observation and `gml_render.c::gml_render_completed_screen_publish` retires the
earlier SCREEN recipe after success. The existing row/channel conversion is reused; failed reads
publish nothing and stale/unbound/lost contexts are refused. Controlled current-context destruction
observes resources and the completed image before deletion. `GmlGpuCounters.completed_frame_*`
separates this traffic from scene checkpoints and content reads. The controls are
`tests/unit/video/gpu/test_gpu.c::completed_frame_snapshot_case` and
`tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_state_preserves_completed_device_case`.
Missing proof, excluded presentation variants and ordinary replay/checkpoint policy retain their
existing paths. Context-access transitions, full frontend qualification and removal of ordinary
maintenance remain open; this transport adds no raster family.

Scene-mode genuine loss is owned by `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_lose_volatile_device`
and `src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_graphics_lose_scene_context`. Device-authoritative numbered
surfaces and targets of discarded queued work become nonexistent; current CPU/state images
survive. Existing surface-free target unwinding and incarnation allocation are reused. Managed
application storage is reinitialised only when unobserved; lost completed device images are
withdrawn rather than replayed. No dead-context callback, checkpoint or deletion is permitted.
The controls are `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_volatile_context_loss_case`,
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::volatile_loss_discards_queued_nested_target`
and `::volatile_loss_recreates_only_unobserved_application`. These are lifecycle controls, not
new raster coverage, ordinary-read removal or whole-frontend qualification. CPU-mode context
recovery, initial adoption, Reset and valid saved-image restoration keep their existing paths.

The later resident prototype supports clear, qualified whole-image quads, and nearest-filtered
interior atlas subrectangles with integer-sized axis-aligned destinations and optional X/Y mirrors.
A narrow ordinary sprite path accepts unit-scale, integer-aligned authored trim when its atlas crop
has only alpha 0 or 255; other texture-stage filters are irrelevant to this unprogrammed base-image
draw, and a binary-alpha crop uses endpoint source-over. Its CPU journal replay rejects any
intermediate-alpha source. These variants are replayable from retained source pixels after context
loss. A separate first-generation filtered path accepts qualified ordinary sprites with
partial alpha, tint, per-draw alpha, authored trim, partial source crops and either scale sign. It preserves snapped
destination coverage and fractional texture phase. For logical padding around a packed atlas
cell, taps crossing a padded edge use physical atlas coordinates on both axes; other taps clamp to
the selected source rectangle. The path requires physical margin pixels where needed, uses
source-over RGB with the application's source-alpha-squared coverage rule, and checkpoints the
device result rather than claiming exact CPU replay. Qualified cardinal and arbitrary rotations
use the same source-over checkpoint with the first-generation CPU's nearest-texel inverse map;
near-zero angles classified as axis draws, special sprite states and unqualified crop geometry
retain the ordered CPU fallback. A separate modern-layer filtered rotation branch uses the CPU's
four-tap inverse map, nonzero-alpha crop bounds and conditional per-row quadrilateral span. It
requires a proven opaque application target and uses the modern screen target's opaque-alpha
blend policy; transparent targets still cross the ordered CPU barrier. Both branches retain
the CPU checkpoint for lost-device reconstruction.
The public `draw_sprite_general` entry selects its source part independently of the authored
sprite origin. Uniform-corner rotation uses the existing rotated CPU blitter or the qualified
resident inverse-map quad. Distinct corner colours use a CPU two-triangle colour interpolation
