The existing full-sprite axis/point/nonzero-rotation function audits require the opt-in scale-bounds companion,3456 RGBA/coverage pairs each across six policies,four owners,GUI,all12 presets and correlated masks/alpha/trim/reflection/scale axes. Report actual GPU work,empty admissions,exact counted CPU debt,detached bytes and numbered states separately; no whole-function/game/speed percentage follows. |

R03-SPR-SCALE-BOUNDS precision debt: first-generation full-axis filtered
destination-colour presets on alpha-preserving targets with alpha writes and
variable source alpha remain ordered CPU. Trim alone does not refuse an
already-qualified opaque source. A paired numbered-target
binary-alpha case measured alpha4 at scale-8,1 under Mesa D3D12;the same input
with alpha writes masked off passes. This is a measured individual-D2 refusal,
not a changed threshold or a native-driver finding. Opaque interiors and RGB-only
masks retain their separately paired packets. Other existing source/program/
depth/preset/geometry refusals remain counted debt,not blanket precision waivers.

The R03-P9-FILTERED-REAL-TEXT arithmetic first uses one cached combined GLES
program for first-generation and modern text, matching the desktop program's
single pass per glyph. A compiler, link or interface failure selects the established
split fallback for the lifetime of that context: separate cached generation programs
write three GPU passes, one RGB channel at a time, with alpha written only with blue.
All fallback passes sample the same bounded pre-glyph device snapshot, preserving
source-over order and alpha. Each complete fallback glyph on a reported V3D renderer,
or when the renderer identity is unavailable, is submitted with `glFlush` before
another pass can sample its target. This prevents the measured lost RGB writes on
translucent V3D sources without a completion wait or CPU pixel read. Other identified
renderers retain the ordered same-context command stream without that per-glyph
submission. The decision is cached per context and recomputed at reset; arithmetic,
snapshots and channel/alpha ordering are unchanged. The general exact GLES program
continues to exclude font arithmetic. `scene_gles_font_programs_case` checks combined
selection across renderer identities and forced split fallback with the same
whole-batch/failure/channel controls. Integer-limb shifts avoid undefined native shift counts,
and a bounded highest-bit search replaces repeated normalisation shifts without
changing rounding. This limits compiler inlining rather than moving text to the
CPU. `tests/unit/video/gpu/test_gpu.c::scene_gles_font_programs_case` covers channel
masks, cached generation selection and partial-pass failure cleanup; the existing
real-device filtered-font comparison remains the pixel qualification.

Surface metadata used to prepare these rules has a distinct non-raster owner:
`common/gml_render_surfaces.c::surface_dimensions` prepares
`::gml_surface_exists`, `::gml_surface_width` and `::gml_surface_height` from
backing/lifetime, extent and resource-authority metadata. These calls issue no GPU
draw or shader and do not consume journals or seal valid pending results. They
preserve invalid-resource and application-publication fences. Actual pixel reads
still use `cpu/gml_render_cpu_surfaces.c::surface_pixels_access` and
`common/gml_render_resources.c::gml_render_resource_acquire_cpu_read` /
`::gml_render_resource_checkpoint`.
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::surface_metadata_preserves_device_authority`
compares authority, versions, replay/checkpoint counts and real pixel-observer
results for application and numbered resources. This metadata rule removes an
unnecessary CPU observer, not the content-program checkpoint contract.

P8-T27 extends the existing `R03-P8-STATE-RETIRE` state/lifetime boundary, not a new raster
rule. `tests/contract/libretro_dynamic_rewind.c::monitor_growth_case` uses the same authored
moving-sprite/audio preparation at a 1920×1080 logical raster, then changes the delivered virtual
monitor to 1280×960. The unchanged CPU scene body and the existing GPU sprite executor/shader from
`R03-SPR-NEAREST` remain responsible for pixels; the test makes a completed frame high-entropy solely
to distinguish the 1-MiB fixed compact ring from acknowledged variable capacity. It checks
frontend size-before-save, no-context callbacks, exact policy-specific restore/continuation,
visible image/audio, accepted scene sprites, Reset and bounded uncompressed copy/restore cost.
Rejected/unsupported variable-size negotiation keeps the fixed slot; only an acknowledged frontend
may grow it. This does not accelerate state copying or assert all Draw/GUI is GPU-resident.

P8-T28 extends that same `R03-P8-STATE-RETIRE` lifetime rule through an unannounced context reset,
not a new GPU raster kernel. `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::context_replacement_reuses_names_case`
uses the unchanged `R03-SPR-NEAREST` CPU/GPU draw preparation, saves and restores with GL calls
forbidden, then resets the fake driver namespace and deliberately reuses an old texture number.
It compares the CPU and GPU-selected images and canonical states across the restored frame and
continuation, requires new GPU sprite work, and rejects deletion of the recycled live texture.
`::refused_context_case` separately covers a named missing entry point, CPU continuation and a
later valid GPU adoption. These are engine/fake-driver lifecycle controls; physical-device pixels
and final frontend cost retain their separately recorded evidence boundaries.

P8-T29 qualifies the per-engine part of `R03-P3-CONTEXT-RESET` and `R03-P8-STATE-RETIRE` without
adding a shader or CPU algorithm. `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::two_live_gpu_engines_case`
interleaves two GPU-selected engines and two CPU controls at different frame positions. Each GPU
engine owns a distinct host resolver result for `glGetError`, framebuffer callback, source texture,
scene counter and canonical state. Resetting then non-current-destroying only one engine must leave
the other's callback dispatch, resource identity, image, state and counted sprite work intact. The
fixture's shared fake driver proves logical isolation and entry-table ownership, not independent
physical GL contexts or final performance; T25's separate real-device comparison owns pixels.

### Selected-surface feedback companion

R03-SURFACE-NEAREST-FUNCTION additionally admits an equal-extent source that
physically aliases its selected target. Flush the ordered prefix, resolve the
actual target resource, reuse the existing GPU-only source snapshot, submit
the consumer and only then retire the scratch image. This is genuine source
feedback, not general destination-reading blending or CPU replay. The unchanged
CPU owner copies its aliased source before rasterization; its Classic pointer-
identity exceptions therefore do not apply to the immutable feedback copy.
Nearest, bounded BOX and hardware surface validators accept this same typed
ARGB scratch source without changing their other geometry or state bounds.

The public surface generator's `feedback` companion requires 4320 correlated
controls over six Classic/Studio policies, five alias owners, world/GUI,
opaque/binary/partial source alpha, both filtering preferences and all twelve
presets. Its finite union is 3527 GPU writers, 685 no-ops and 108 counted CPU
boundaries, never 4320 GPU writers or whole-function coverage. Qualified
opaque/binary integral nearest endpoints require EXACT RGBA0; other admitted
draws require TOLERANCE±2, with exact outside coverage and disabled channels.
Independent CPU support preserves the original coordinate mapping and the
copied source identity. Admitted draws allow one GPU source copy and no
ordinary read, mask, RGB query, replay or CPU-shadow mutation. Six numbered
actual-pixel save/load/save controls and detached actual-byte seals are gates.
Alpha-one, outside-one, tolerance-three and an actually corrupted GPU source
copy must fail in pixels. Resource tests cover pending clears, all owner
generations, missing copy support, safe scratch initialization retry and
uncertain consumer invalidation without queued retired pointers.

The 108 boundary arguments/observations remain reported separately. In
particular, zero-opacity destination factors are not assumed empty and stay
on their counted CPU owner. Mismatched aliased extents/pitches, phase planes,
unqualified presentation/canvas/program/depth/fog and other original sampler
or precision exclusions remain debt. Existing ordinary, borrowed, ordered,
canvas, rotated and content-program companions retain their own contracts.

### Independent numbered surface copies

R03-SURFACE-NEAREST-FUNCTION also accepts an already resident numbered child
selected before any world/SCREEN scene. Scene policy must be enabled and the
child's resident authority/stack must be valid;this never opens its CPU parent
or changes the global scene-pass flag. The public full-source surface port
reuses its existing nearest,filtered and qualified program helpers. Their
source,state,precision and program fences remain unchanged;this is not new
content-program execution or a new surface-copy algorithm.
The existing surface-function matrix's independent-child overlay retains its
CPU oracle and exact support,all ordinary owners/samplers/presets,state and
transport gates. Its numbered controls require closed global passes,actual
detached bytes and restoration of the untouched CPU parent. Disabled unknown
programs additionally retain the CPU passthrough policy and readable selection.
The old independent-target sprite,canvas,order and source neighbours remain
gates;unqualified source/precision/program domains stay explicit CPU debt.

### Selected-sprite source area

Selected1:1 crops at finite fractional anchors share the ordinary unit-copy
classification. The CPU snaps that origin before its unit writer;the authored
fraction does not select a separate large-axis algorithm. This classification
only chooses the existing area/alpha guards,not a new sampler phase. Whole
sprite,tiled and replacement ports keep their previous anchor requirement.
The shared part/general generator's large-source-unit and
large-source-unit-presets companions pair the fractional anchors and a white
1/42 unit draw with the existing policy,target,alpha,filter and preset axes.
Large-visible unit alpha8 and first-generation written-alpha fences remain
explicit CPU boundaries,as do programs,mutable sources and unqualified
precision domains. Finite controls are not a whole-function denominator.
The legacy unit fallback must not bypass the first-generation large-unit
boundary:the paired white partial-alpha control exposes an alpha138 error
despite RGB differences of1. Keep that state on the counted CPU owner until
its vector/tail written-alpha policy has a qualified GPU equivalent;this is
pending executor debt,not an unavoidable precision exception.

R03-SPR-PART-FUNCTION keeps the nonunit selected source on the CPU point geometry
owner even above131072 projected source pixels. The whole-sprite API's
separate large-area rotated owner is not selected by a part draw. Only the
shared area rejection is removed for that existing nonunit selected-part packet;
separately qualified unit-copy routes and their alpha fences retain priority.
source,coordinate,phase,program and precision bounds remain in force.
The paired large-source and large-source-presets companions require each
complete7056-control union across six policies/four targets,including clipped
640x320 sources,normal and all twelve presets,partial draw/source alpha and
signed/fractional axes. Independent CPU coverage,RGBA EXACT0/individual2,
source reuse,zero ordinary transport and exact detached bytes/numbered states
remain gates. Filtered/other-function owners are unchanged;this finite proof
is not a semantic whole-function or game percentage.

### Invalid sprite-part selections

The early rejection applies only to an active resident pass. The default CPU
and an already selected software suffix retain their original entry order.

| Rule | Contract | Shared preparation | CPU reference | GPU execution | Scope and evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R03-SPR-PART-INVALID-RESOURCE | EXACT RGBA0,empty coverage and unchanged draw state | `common/gml_render_sprites.c::gml_draw_sprite_part_ext` rejects a negative or upper-bound sprite index before acquiring target authority; the uniform unrotated general alias shares this entry | The unchanged CPU suffix already returns before its pixel writer for the same invalid resource | No submission,source upload,snapshot,replay or GPU credit;existing ordered and pending-fill prefixes remain resident | All ordinary owners and generation policies,regardless of filter,blend,tint,alpha,mask or program state. This is no-write qualification,not a new visible sprite executor or whole-function closure. `tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::resident_invalid_sprite_part_preserves_pending_work` checks both aliases,all twelve presets/sixteen masks,both filters,world/GUI mapping and pending authority without device calls. device comparison R03-SPR-PART-INVALID-RESOURCE uses sprite-part-function profile invalid-resource:7056 correlated CPU/GPU pairs,negative and upper-bound indices,named/cached simple/ext and direct/general ports,EXACT RGBA0,independent empty coverage,unchanged metadata/shadow,zero ordinary reads/copies and36 numbered actual-byte state pairs. Alpha-one and outside-one negatives and the unchanged ordinary part neighbour remain required |

## Frame CPU-access and resident-source audit

This is the live P7 frame-coherence map, not the historical P1 classification.
The class names describe the access actually performed, not the name of its
public API. A CPU read/write is ordered after queued scene work. The remaining
cost and capability limits are explicit below; a CPU fallback is not counted as
GPU execution.

The filtered Classic retained-Step actual-host neighbour remains unqualified:
CPU/device RGB differs by up to207 levels, identically with and without the
numbered-GUI selection change. Twenty delivered same-backend checkpoint PNGs
and the six reset/transport runtime traces are unchanged by that change.
This is existing filtered-presentation debt, not tolerant coverage or a passing
pixel control. The nearest doubled/native/early/base-writer neighbours own
their separate exact comparisons; no filtered admission is added here.

| Access owner and grouped variants | Class and coherence boundary | Current limit or next owner |
|---|---|---|
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_begin`; `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_begin_retaining_target`; resident owned/base begins | Metadata and pass selection after scene/rotation flush; retained stack, fills and target identity are preserved or reject resident begin. `gml_render_begin_owned_application` is a CPU write with resource acquire/commit; `gml_render_begin_resident_owned_application`, `gml_render_begin_resident_base` and `gml_render_begin_resident_base_retaining_target` select the qualified device target. The retaining base variant preserves a positive classic Step target while replacing its base dimensions/camera. GUI also retains the qualified numbered target when the logical root owns an independent vertical phase plane; selecting the physical SCREEN base is not a CPU pixel write. | Unsupported interpolated, canvas and special states deliberately use the CPU pass. A real screen CPU consumer must materialise the prior deferred presentation before an ordinary begin resets it. The doubled-Step engine counter control rejects implicit steady-frame reads; actual-device pairs and writer neighbours separately own pixels and state. |
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_application_surface_bind`, `::gml_render_application_surface_select_owned`, and `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_application_surface_publish` | Metadata; a changed binding flushes queued scene work. `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::application_surface_resize` follows R03-P5-SURFACE-RESIZE: GPU-local rectangular migration for device-only pixels, otherwise the existing CPU copy with observation when needed; `gml_render_application_surface_owned_clear` and `::gml_render_application_surface_owned_view` are CPU writes with acquire/commit. | Surface 0 can be borrowed, disabled or independently resized. Do not infer ownership from its numeric ID. |
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_application_surface_owned_read`, `::gml_render_target_pixel`; `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_surface_pixels_read`; `cpu/gml_render_cpu_surfaces.c::surface_pixels_observe` | CPU reads with scene flush and resource acquire; target reads also resolve pending Draw/fill. `cpu/gml_render_cpu_surfaces.c::surface_pixels` is a CPU write with acquire/commit and suspended-fill realization. | Numbered and application resources remain coherent, but a non-replayable device result can require a full checkpoint. `gml_surface_exists`, `gml_surface_width` and `gml_surface_height` use `surface_dimensions` metadata in the common facade and do not observe pixel bytes. A request, a CPU acquisition and an actual device read remain distinct diagnostic quantities. |
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_sample_planes_update`; renderer coverage/target metrics; immutable atlas and runtime image source selection | Metadata or read-only asset borrow, not a framebuffer borrow. The sample-plane pointers are attached to the pass; their producers write the classic/application phase arrays before the CPU sample that consumes them. | An asset reference does not make a mutable target current. Runtime page/version rules are separate P5/P6 rows above. |
| `cpu/gml_render_cpu_backend.c::gml_render_cpu_backend_prepare_draw_view`, `cpu/gml_render_cpu_primitives.c` particle leaves and `src/video/software3d/` draws | CPU writes through the resident CPU barrier and preserved backend draw view; numbered targets use their surface facade. Opt-in diagnostics mark actual software-3D color writes and count hidden-surface depth tests separately from ordinary particle/software draws. | Ordered CPU paths, not device kernels. Only actual depth-tested writes enter the depth exception counter; an active 3D flag alone grants no exception. `boundary.dispatch_accounting` compares both policies and diagnostics on/off pixels. The complete-load dispatch footer described in DIAGNOSTICS.md includes ordinary rasters, retained replay and composition; it is accounting, not a claim of zero fallback or driver qualification. |
| `src/core/engine_presentation.c::render_multiview_application`, `::seed_view_rect`, `::compose_view_rect`; `gml_render.c::gml_render_surface_mirror_pixels` | R03-MULTIVIEW-COMPOSITION replaces admitted raw clear/seed/copy work with exact checkpointed GPU operations. Explicit Root/view/SCREEN registrations retain independent raw-linear history. Excluded or interrupted passes observe actual resources before the unchanged CPU suffix. The mirror still writes a numbered surface through its own target facade before same-frame GUI readers. | R03-P7-MULTIVIEW-SEED/RETAINED/SURFACE keep the historical CPU/replay controls and special-target boundaries; they do not independently imply GPU compositor admission. Remaining whole-view observations, assigned-view exclusions and other presentation transfers stay explicit P9 debt. |
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_flush_pending_fill`, `::gml_render_flush_pending_underlay`; `common/gml_render_presentation.c::render_record_deferred_underlay`, `::render_present_first_generation`; rotated-batch flush | A qualified fill is an accelerated neutral clear; otherwise the target is acquired for a CPU write. Deferred terminal presentation records metadata and a frame-local source borrow; CPU execution acquires the resident source in `cpu/gml_render_cpu_presentation.c::gml_render_cpu_present_image`. R03-P9-AUTOMATIC-UNDERLAY instead composes an unmasked current application into the owned resident SCREEN before following atlas work, with byte-free exact source retention in R03-P9-REPLAY-SOURCE. The rotated queue flushes before a borrow or target change. First-generation mask-covered underlay still acquires the current CPU resource before its temporary alpha borrow and restores the original bytes without a version change. | R03-P7-UNDERLAY-ALPHA-BORROW remains the mask exclusion's GPU-current resource guard, not acceleration of that alpha borrow. Unqualified sources/state/geometry retain CPU composition; a non-replayable source uses its mandatory checkpoint. |
| `src/core/engine.c::engine_run_frame` world/view/GUI, centered-aspect crop, indirect-GUI scaler, empty-screen fallback; `src/core/engine_presentation.c::draw_game_cursor` and `::classic_transition_start` | Complete current writer-free indirect GUI can defer nearest enlargement or box reduction/mixed axes without a CPU borrow through `gml_render_defer_resident_screen_upscale`. Centered/excluded GUI materialises first. A cursor over the same current completed SCREEN can retain its ordinary sprite suffix on device; deferred/unwitnessed/different targets and transition snapshots still materialise before CPU draw/copy. Empty-screen fallback is skipped for a deferred full-target presentation. | R03-P7-INDIRECT-GUI owns scaling and exact canonical reconstruction; R03-P7-COMPLETED-CURSOR owns only direct SCREEN rebind. R03-P7-SCREEN-CPU-BORROW retains the explicit late CPU boundaries. |
| `src/core/engine_presentation.c::resolve_host_frame`, `::engine_build_host_plan`, `::present_shift_frame`; `src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_present_hardware_canvas` | CPU read after `engine_materialize_completed_frame`, followed by CPU plan/copy or a complete-frame upload. Resident SCREEN and host-canvas presentations avoid it when their source and plan qualify. | Host canvas CRT/sharp-bilinear, nonresident box, crop, presentation shift and other nonresident plans still materialise/transport. These are measured P7 fallback policies and unresolved P9 ordinary-route transfer costs. |
| `src/core/engine_state.c::state_write_completed_frame`, `src/core/engine_presentation.c::log_present_pass`, coverage diagnostics | CPU read after explicit completed-frame materialisation or resource facade acquisition. Diagnostics that scan pixels are opt-in, not evidence of ordinary-route residency. | P8 must qualify frontend state/ring call ordering; P9 measures materialisation cost. |

`GmlPlanImage.cpu_pixels` is a frame-local CPU borrow owned by the caller; a
retained host plan remains valid only while its owner retains that allocation.
`GmlPlanImage.resident_image` is a context-local resource reference with
incarnation and content version. Today `common/gml_render_plan.c::gml_render_plan_validate`
admits it for an opaque APPLICATION/application-surface or SCREEN/completed-frame
`BLIT_OPAQUE_NEAREST` source, with matching typed class and current version;
`gpu/opengl/gml_render_gpu_opengl_plan.c::gml_gpu_opengl_execute` resolves the
exact current texture, while `cpu/gml_render_cpu_plan.c::gml_render_cpu_plan_execute_pooled`
refuses a resident-only image. The selected resident underlay path is
`gml_render.c::gml_render_defer_resident_underlay` into
`src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_present_hardware_screen`, with CPU replay
through `gml_render_cpu_present_image` when the device is unavailable. Direct completed SCREEN
presentation and `gml_render_defer_resident_screen_upscale` use the same typed plan;
their canonical observers resolve the SCREEN resource, not the application substitute.

| Presentation source and operation | Device capability today | Correct fallback and remaining owner |
|---|---|---|
| Opaque owned application, deferred nearest underlay | Resident application image → exact-version GPU nearest plan; no complete source upload | CPU materialise/replay on refusal or context loss. |
| Unmasked current owned application, automatic underlay before filtered SCREEN/GUI atlas work | R03-P9-AUTOMATIC-UNDERLAY uses the existing ordered exact surface quad. A witnessed on-demand device may upload an already committed CPU-current source at 1:1 extents without reading SCREEN; legacy exact recipes and GPU-source scaling remain unchanged. | Scaled CPU-current source, mask/phase, special state, open source writer, unavailable witness, borrowed/stale source or excluded footprint remains the original CPU compositor. Resident pixels still require observation at explicit consumers; no automatic frame checkpoint is implied. |
| Current completed SCREEN or writer-free indirect GUI with nonshrinking axes | Typed resident SCREEN → exact-version GPU nearest plan, using the existing axis-map sampler without complete CPU transport | CPU materialise/replay on device refusal/loss; a genuine writer invalidates residence. Enlargement canonical pixels retain their original zero XRGB high byte. |
| Borrowed/disabled application without a current resident target, multiview scratch and excluded indirect GUI | Only separately qualified scene draws and current source versions are GPU work | CPU barrier/composition and complete presentation transport; reduction, writer suffix, centered aspect, canvas, shift and crop remain explicit exclusions, not completed P9 residency. |
| CPU-current application or completed frame, nearest/box/sharp-bilinear host plan | CPU source upload or CPU plan; box/sharp require their established CPU kernel to preserve exact pixels | P7 fallback correctness is measured. Resident SCREEN box follows R03-P9-RESIDENT-HOST-FIT (TOLERANCE±2); sharp/crop/shift remain open. |
| Content shader terminal plan | GPU program when qualified, with a checkpoint/readback where required | Ineligible program uses the documented CPU/plain path. P7-S1-D has a qualified natural shader/original comparison; P8 checks state ordering and P9 costs. |

The same `resident_identity_screen_gui` predicate also admits an identity-geometry sprite on a
direct, disabled-application GUI `SCREEN` target. `engine_run_frame` already selected
`gml_render_begin_resident_base` for that target before R03-P7-INDIRECT-GUI; its sprite now follows
`resident_integer_sprite` / `gml_render_resident_atlas_crop`, the original
`gml_render_cpu_blit_sprite_ext_resolved` fallback, the counted textured-quad executor and
`R03_SHADER_DIRECT_TEXTURE` branch named in that row. The direct variant in
`resident_indirect_gui_case` compares full CPU/scene output bytes, a visible sprite, counted clear
and textured-quad work, repeated frames and context loss. This does not qualify arbitrary direct
GUI operations: transformed GUI geometry retains its CPU path. The additional
R03-P9-GUI-FILTERED-ATLAS row maps identity-screen filtered atlas preparation,
screen alpha policy and real shader branches. R03-P9-GUI-MAPPED-SPRITE additionally maps
owned logical GUI scaling to the unchanged ordinary/part and distinct stretched sprite
rules, including their alpha and checkpoint/replay limits. R03-RESIDENT-PRESENT separately qualifies
direct completed SCREEN presentation, and R03-P7-INDIRECT-GUI maps writer-free
terminal enlargement and integer-footprint reduction/mixed axes; neither follows merely from sprite counts. The
R03-P7-VIEWLESS-BASE row's former broad indirect-GUI exclusion is superseded only for these
qualified GUI rules; its viewless-world and borrowed-surface-0 claims are unchanged.
R03-P9-DIRECT-AUTOMATIC-GUI additionally prepares the direct SCREEN for a viewless or single-view
nonclassic owned application with automatic drawing enabled. Its pending underlay
and first qualified stretched draw consume the existing surface and sprite rules,
not an application-image substitute. Classic and multiview direct admission remain
unchanged; covered-alpha canonical checkpoints and excluded writers remain explicit.
R03-P9-PRE-DRAW-SCREEN admits the existing SCREEN resource for nonclassic,
noncomposed, unforced OpenGL Pre-Draw. The implicit clear remains EXACT; draws
retain their existing per-family EXACT or TOLERANCE limits and checked barriers.
The event still precedes view selection and its queue flushes before geometry
updates. Pending state, stacked targets and unavailable resources retain checked
CPU fallback. `test_graphics_state.c::resident_pre_draw_screen_case` pairs the
CPU and selected paths, including the event's view mutation, zero ordinary CPU
work and exact canonical state. `test_gpu_scene_route --keep-pre-draw` exports
the same neutral route for real-device comparison. It does not qualify previously excluded sprites
or establish complete real-device image coverage.
CPU materialization of a deferred presentation publishes its completed target,
retiring any earlier SCREEN resource. A later canonical observer must not replay
that resource's preceding clear over the newly materialized image. This ownership
transition adds no per-frame readback and does not alter numbered-surface alpha.
The historical R03-BG-DIRECT-NEAREST-COPY row's additive exclusion is superseded for the
qualified additive subset by R03-BG-ADDITIVE. Other additive variants still take its stated
ordered CPU fallback.

The neutral scene-op ledger classifies its unselected dedicated triangle opcode
with the real CPU fallback; its name must not be used to infer that the
qualified positioned quads are absent.
Conversely, resident texture sampling and flat-region primitive paint do not imply complete P6
primitive/3D coverage or P7 shaders/effects are delivered. Any later qualification updates
the affected row or adds a rule group when its actual backend branch and
comparison are available.

## Function dispatch inventory (D8 foundation)

This inventory is maintained by `tests/architecture/check_render_operations.py`, already
part of `make architecture-check`. It discovers public drawing facades, resident
preparators, software raster/vertex/model entries and the existing OpenGL draw boundaries
from their C definitions, independently of these rows. It rejects missing/extra entries,
a changed submission route, or a changed definition without an updated row.
The revision is a SHA-256 of C tokens excluding comments, not a pixel expectation.

`PARTIAL` means only that a conservative call graph reaches the named typed admission
port. It does **not** mean that the draw is admitted in every state, that the device accepts
it, or that a whole game is GPU-resident. CPU preparation/observation/flush boundaries
are cut from the graph: completing a prior GPU prefix never credits a later CPU writer.
`DEBT` records an ordinary CPU drawing route. `HELPER` records the enumerated existing
admission/CPU-handoff utilities, not an exception that may hide a new drawing API.
`DEFERRED` records the two existing host-presentation adapters. `DEVICE` records
execution/encoding boundaries, not complete support for every payload.

This is the function-level foundation, **not yet the complete D8 gate**. Builtin API
adapters/aliases, resolved packet/shader variants and their complete paired CPU/GPU
control bindings still require mechanical coverage before D8 is declared complete.
The existing R03 map above remains the sole owner of qualified variant behavior,
EXACT/TOLERANCE bounds and known exclusions. No renderer policy rule is claimed to
be enforced by this inventory alone.

R03-TEXT-NEAREST-FUNCTION requires complete nearest public-string paired proof
before qualification. EXACT RGBA0 covers opaque/binary endpoints and disabled
replacement; partial source/draw alpha and inverse blend require TOLERANCE±2
with exact coverage. The bounded immutable atlas owner offers signed nearest
scales, all ordinary destinations, Classic/Studio, mapped world/GUI, tint and
raw cutout without a new shader, destination snapshot, ordinary observation or
CPU replay. Classic logical TEXT stays nearest even with presentation interpolation
enabled; the caller's flag is unchanged. Complete base/layout/bounds controls for
each changed Classic policy and unchanged Studio neighbours qualify this extension.
Studio logical filtering and all special-state guards remain unchanged.
Existing cheap/runtime packets stay first. Detached real glyphs
use conservative alpha metadata; copied sprite cells retain their distinct
CPU cache phase. `common/gml_render_text.c::draw_text_real_plain` and
`common/gml_render_text.c::draw_text_transformed_font` preserve the CPU layout;
`common/gml_render_sprites.c::gml_render_resident_font_glyph` and
`common/gml_render_sprites.c::gml_render_resident_sprite_font_glyph` prepare
cells through the existing atlas packet. `cpu/gml_render_cpu_blit.c::blit`
remains the reference; `gpu/opengl/gml_gpu_opengl_batch.c::integer_function_sprite_valid`
admits the bounded packet. The admission/authority unit
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::resident_nearest_text_function`
does not prove pixels: the public-string device comparison must establish the
font/source/draw-alpha/tint/cutout/blend/scale/layout/cache matrix and rejecting
negatives, ordinary zero transport and exact detached actual bytes; a subset
or admission pass cannot qualify the rule. The new owner
rejects active maximised/offset GUI and copied cells whose physical and logical
dimensions differ. Existing qualified owners or ordered CPU debt remain for
these cases; this does not assert that they cannot meet D2. Other blends,
filter/rotation, runtime/SDF/subpixel, shaders/masks/fog/depth, auxiliary planes,
oversized geometry and unresolved GUI authority/presentation remain explicit
ordered CPU debt, not an assertion that those variants cannot meet D2.

R03-SPR-CPU-PASSTHROUGH is a cross-cutting ordinary sprite variant. Older
program-refusal notes below retain recognized effects and applicable external
executors;only the existing CPU-ignore policy is additionally qualified. This
does not claim execution of the selected content program.

R03-CLASSIC-STEP-COMPOSITION's `retained-vertical` companion adds432 complete
public pairs with an actually retained root resource before numbered/GUI draws.
Real/sprite axis and rotated glyphs, selected backgrounds and selected tiles
use their existing ordinary owner when the root plane is inactive on that binding.
288 endpoints require EXACT RGBA0 and144 partial-alpha pairs allow individual2;
root-peer bytes/version, no-write coverage and detached actual bytes stay exact.
Zero ordinary reads/masks/queries/snapshots/replay and all three existing
numbered-phase comparator negatives remain required. The resource fixture
`paired_root_numbered_ports_keep_peer_inert` owns admission/authority only.
Active-root, aliased-base, application-zero and interpolated states keep their
existing paired owner or rejection. No CPU writer, shader or state schema changes.
An absent bound vertical plane with a retained peer keeps its existing CPU
boundary; the numbered resource fixture additionally checks that refusal.

## Drawing API dispatch inventory (D8 foundation)

The same architecture checker reuses the canonical builtin registry and lexer to discover
exact drawing spellings, shared-ID aliases, direct cached switch arms, ordinary string
adapters and contextual 3D adapters. Explicit literal delegation by legacy drawing actions
is followed; a generic builtin/VM router is never credited with every renderer callee.
Non-paint getters/setters are excluded unless their actual adapter reaches a registered
drawing facade. Surface target/lifetime APIs that can compose an underlay are therefore
present. Registry identity includes ID, numeric value, owner, stage, cache policy and alias
status. Source revisions cover the selected adapter arms, not whole unrelated builtin files.

`PARTIAL` means a possible route to a bounded facade, not all-state admission or an API-wide
pixel qualification. `DEBT` includes ordinary software drawing **and explicitly identified
indirect executor-binding debt**. Accumulating a vertex paints nothing; a deferred clear or
VM event is not measured CPU work simply because its terminal edge is not bound here.
Runtime GPU/fallback counters, rather than these structural counts, own N/40.

This extends the function foundation with 119 spellings (100 partial routes,
19 debt rows). Missing/duplicate spellings, changed exact-ID/cache/alias ownership,
changed fast/slow/contextual arms, changed possible facade or stale revision fail the gate.
Comments do not change revisions; string literals and compound operators do.
Resolved packet/shader variants and their complete live CPU/GPU paired controls are still
pending. This is not D8 closure and does not authorise its AGENTS rule or another family lot.

<!-- D8-API:END -->

## Historical P1 CPU-milestone snapshot (not current support)

| Neutral ID | Shared rule owner | Current CPU execution anchor | OpenGL/OpenGL ES target at P1 | Barrier or fallback |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `TEXTURED_QUAD` | Sprite, background, tile and text facades; frame/region and transform | `gml_render_cpu_blit_tpag_part` and connected runtime-image blitters | `gml_render_gpu_opengl_draw_textured_quad` — planned | Pixel reads require the latest image version before CPU sampling. |
| `TEXTURED_TRIANGLES` | Positioned-sprite and skeletal geometry | `gml_render_cpu_blit_sprite_pos_resolved` and skeletal raster closure | `gml_render_gpu_opengl_draw_textured_triangles` — planned | Preserve triangle/UV order, camera phase and source lifetime. |
| `COLOURED_PRIMITIVE` | `common/gml_render_primitives.c` shape/rectangle policy | `gml_render_cpu_rectangle` and connected primitive leaves | `gml_render_gpu_opengl_draw_primitive` — planned | CPU coverage remains the checkpoint for any unqualified shape. |
| `CLEAR` | Target/colour-write and deferred-fill rules | `gml_render_clear` | `gml_render_gpu_opengl_clear` — planned | Flush pending work at target and mask boundaries. |
| `COPY_IMAGE` | Surface IDs, versions, aliases and target stack | `gml_surface_copy` and copy-part closure | `gml_render_gpu_opengl_copy_image` — qualified bounded raw blits | Self/overlapping copies use one device source snapshot; unsupported resource/history states retain the original CPU kernels. |
| `COMPOSE_SURFACE` | Shared surface request and screen-raster policy | `gml_render_cpu_draw_surface_stretched_impl` and part/rotated kernels | `gml_render_gpu_opengl_compose_surface` — planned | Resolve current source version, target alias and suspended fill. |
| `LAYER_EFFECT` | Layer capture and recognized-family policy | `gml_render_cpu_layer_filter_end` | `gml_render_gpu_opengl_apply_effect` — planned | Unsupported families fall back as a whole pass, preserving order. |
| `PRESENT_IMAGE` | Presentation region, axes and host eligibility | `gml_render_cpu_present_image` | `gml_render_gpu_opengl_present_image` — planned | Readback only when the frontend, state or CPU consumer requires pixels. |
| `CONTENT_SHADER_DRAW` | Shader recognition, uniforms, sampler and host-handoff policy | `gml_render_cpu_shader_pass_surface` — explicit host-program/CPU-composition fallback | Resident content-program pass — planned | No invented exact software interpreter; checkpoint the produced pixels and retain the established host handoff. |
| `SOFTWARE3D_PASS` | Existing software-3D projection, depth and draw-state policy | `gml_d3_draw_sprite_2d` and connected software-3D leaves | Explicit CPU fallback; no accelerated 3D promise | Flush GPU work, synchronize the target for CPU, then upload only changed content before later GPU work. |

The target executor naming pair for the first eight rows is
`gml_render_cpu_<suffix>` / `gml_render_gpu_opengl_<suffix>`, with suffixes
`draw_textured_quad`, `draw_textured_triangles`, `draw_primitive`, `clear`,
`copy_image`, `compose_surface`, `apply_effect`, and `present_image`.
The current CPU anchors above are the preserved implementation bodies; P1 does
not add forwarding aliases or change the call graph merely to give those bodies
new names. New executor entries introduced with the scene dispatcher use the
paired names and real work. The coarse render plan is a separate contract:
`gml_render_cpu_plan_execute` already executes it, while the existing OpenGL
plan execution is extracted under `gml_render_gpu_opengl_plan_execute` in P3.

Rule ownership is deliberate: a geometry or authored-source fix belongs in
`common/`, a sampling/coverage fix in `cpu/` and its qualified GPU counterpart,
and an OpenGL context/FBO/program fix only in `gpu/opengl/`. The one
`GmlRender` record, resource versions and canonical state remain shared; no
backend gets a second logical surface or sprite table. CPU reads require a
barrier from the newest GPU version. CPU writes commit a new version before a
later GPU read. Target changes, feedback/aliasing, shader host handoffs,
fallback, presentation, Reset and context loss are explicit synchronization
boundaries, not implicit per-frame full-frame transfers.

For diagnostics, a neutral report should identify the operation ID, selected
executor or fallback, reason, source/target version, and transfer count. For
example, a synthetic `TEXTURED_QUAD` after a pixel read would record the CPU
checkpoint and one resumed GPU upload; a `SOFTWARE3D_PASS` between two GPU
quads would record a full-pass CPU fallback with the required barriers. A
`planned` row is never reported as an executed GPU operation. Do not call an
existing hybrid plan pass proof that ordinary scene drawing is resident.


## Optional draw-omission policy

The owner-selected `OPENGL_NO_FALLBACK` session policy omits unqualified software
scene draws and safe pre-write device refusals. This deliberate coverage exception
is not D2 qualification: skipped draws are counted separately and never credited
as GPU or CPU execution. CPU and ordinary OpenGL-with-fallback retain their exact
existing executors. The draw-only gate does not disable pixel/source observations,
resource creation, target-stack restoration, serialization or context recovery.
Observers see the partially rendered image; game logic that depends on an omitted
pixel may therefore differ. Device errors with possible writes remain errors;
they are never treated as harmless omissions.

| Variant | CPU | OpenGL with CPU fallback | OpenGL draw omission | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Unsupported scene draw | Existing executor | Existing counted CPU fallback | Deliberate skipped draw | Explicit optional coverage tradeoff; no D2 or whole-game credit |
| Pixel/resource/state observation | Existing operation | Existing observation | Existing observation | Required game/host operation,not a drawing fallback |


Canonical immutable atlas crops retain the unchanged coarse-mip reduction result in a small
derived cache on their texture-page metadata. Both CPU and GPU coarse-veil preparation reuse
that result. The key includes the source address, dimensions, crop, incarnation and version;
mutable font pages and detached views always reduce afresh, and an allocation failure is never
cached as a colour. No raster arithmetic, placement, shader, blend or serialized state bytes
change. `immutable_coarse_texel_cache` checks exact cold/warm channels and invalidation alongside
the existing public coarse-veil controls.
